<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 260C. JUVENILE SAFETY AND PLACEMENT</h2>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>A transfer of a power or responsibility in this chapter to the Department of Children, Youth, and Families is effective upon notice of the commissioner of children, youth, and families to the commissioners of administration, management and budget, and other relevant departments along with the secretary of the senate, the chief clerk of the house of representatives, and the chairs and ranking minority members of the relevant legislative committees and divisions. Laws 2024, chapter 80, article 8, section 72, Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 12, section 30.</p>
      
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>A transfer of a power or responsibility in this chapter to the Department of Children, Youth, and Families is effective upon notice of the commissioner of children, youth, and families to the commissioners of administration, management and budget, and other relevant departments along with the secretary of the senate, the chief clerk of the house of representatives, and the chairs and ranking minority members of the relevant legislative committees and divisions. Laws 2024, chapter 80, article 8, section 72, Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 12, section 30.</p>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.0001"><b>260C.0001</b> MS 2006 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.001">15.001</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">GENERAL PROVISIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.001">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.001 TITLE, INTENT, AND CONSTRUCTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.001.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation; scope.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.001">260C.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.637">260C.637</a> may be cited as the juvenile protection provisions of the Juvenile Court Act.</p>
          <p>(b) Juvenile protection proceedings include:</p>
          <p>(1) a child in need of protection or services matters;</p>
          <p>(2) permanency matters, including termination of parental rights;</p>
          <p>(3) postpermanency reviews under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.317">260C.317</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) adoption matters including posttermination of parental rights proceedings that review the responsible social services agency's reasonable efforts to finalize adoption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.001.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Juvenile protection proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The paramount consideration in all juvenile protection proceedings is the health, safety, and best interests of the child. In proceedings involving an American Indian child, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.8">260.755, subdivision 8</a>, the best interests of the child must be determined consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.751">260.751</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.835">260.835</a> and the Indian Child Welfare Act, United States Code, title 25, sections 1901 to 1923.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The purpose of the laws relating to juvenile protection proceedings is:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) to secure for each child under the jurisdiction of the court, the care and guidance, preferably in the child's own home, as will best serve the spiritual, emotional, mental, and physical welfare of the child;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) to provide judicial procedures that protect the welfare of the child;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) to preserve and strengthen the child's family ties whenever possible and in the child's best interests, removing the child from the custody of parents only when the child's welfare or safety cannot be adequately safeguarded without removal;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) to ensure that when removal from the child's own family is necessary and in the child's best interests, the responsible social services agency has legal responsibility for the child removal either:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) pursuant to a voluntary placement agreement between the child's parent or guardian or the child, when the child is over age 18, and the responsible social services agency; or</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) by court order pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.151#stat.260C.151.6">260C.151, subdivision 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.178">260C.178</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201">260C.201</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325">260C.325</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) to ensure that, when placement is pursuant to court order, the court order removing the child or continuing the child in foster care contains an individualized determination that placement is in the best interests of the child that coincides with the actual removal of the child;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) to ensure that when the child is removed, the child's care and discipline is, as nearly as possible, equivalent to that which should have been given by the parents and is either in:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) the home of a noncustodial parent pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.178">260C.178</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201">260C.201</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (a), clause (1);</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) the home of a relative pursuant to emergency placement by the responsible social services agency under chapter 245A; or</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) foster care licensed under chapter 245A; and</p>
          <p>(7) to ensure appropriate permanency planning for children in foster care including:</p>
          <p>(i) unless reunification is not required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.012">260.012</a>, developing a permanency plan for the child that includes a primary plan for reunification with the child's parent or guardian and a secondary plan for an alternative, legally permanent home for the child in the event reunification cannot be achieved in a timely manner;</p>
          <p>(ii) identifying, locating, and assessing both parents of the child as soon as possible and offering reunification services to both parents of the child as required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.012">260.012</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.219">260C.219</a>;</p>
          <p>(iii) identifying, locating, and notifying relatives of both parents of the child according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>;</p>
          <p>(iv) making a placement with a family that will commit to being the legally permanent home for the child in the event reunification cannot occur at the earliest possible time while at the same time actively supporting the reunification plan; and</p>
          <p>(v) returning the child home with supports and services, as soon as return is safe for the child, or when safe return cannot be timely achieved, moving to finalize another legally permanent home for the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.001.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Permanency, termination of parental rights, and adoption.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of the laws relating to permanency, termination of parental rights, and children who come under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families is to ensure that:</p>
          <p>(1) when required and appropriate, reasonable efforts have been made by the social services agency to reunite the child with the child's parents in a home that is safe and permanent;</p>
          <p>(2) if placement with the parents is not reasonably foreseeable, to secure for the child a safe and permanent placement according to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 2, preferably with a relative through an adoption or a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody or, if that is not possible or in the best interests of the child, with a nonrelative caregiver through adoption; and</p>
          <p>(3) when a child is under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families, reasonable efforts are made to finalize an adoptive home for the child in a timely manner.</p>
          <p>Nothing in this section requires reasonable efforts to prevent placement or to reunify the child with the parent or guardian to be made in circumstances where the court has determined that the child has been subjected to egregious harm, when the child is an abandoned infant, the parent has involuntarily lost custody of another child through a proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.4">260C.515, subdivision 4</a>, or similar law of another state, the parental rights of the parent to a sibling have been involuntarily terminated, or the court has determined that reasonable efforts or further reasonable efforts to reunify the child with the parent or guardian would be futile.</p>
          <p>The paramount consideration in all proceedings for permanent placement of the child under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>, or the termination of parental rights is the best interests of the child. In proceedings involving an American Indian child, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.8">260.755, subdivision 8</a>, the best interests of the child must be determined consistent with the Indian Child Welfare Act of 1978, United States Code, title 25, section 1901, et seq.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.001.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Construction.</span></h2>
          <p>The laws relating to the juvenile protection proceedings shall be liberally construed to carry out these purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.001.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.1.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 1</a>; art 4 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.8.41.0">1999 c 245 art 8 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/159/#laws.2.12.0">2005 c 159 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/361/#laws.6.24.0">2008 c 361 art 6 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.2.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 2; art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.8.4.0">2022 c 98 art 8 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.007">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.007 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this chapter, the terms defined in this section have the same meanings given to them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Agency" means the responsible social services agency or a licensed child-placing agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Case plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Case plan" means any plan for the delivery of services to a child and parent or guardian, or, when reunification is not required, the child alone, that is developed according to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.19">245.4871, subdivision 19</a> or 21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.492#stat.245.492.16">245.492, subdivision 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.26">260E.26</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Child.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Child" means an individual under 18 years of age. For purposes of this chapter and chapter 260D, child also includes individuals under age 21 who are in foster care pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.451">260C.451</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Child abuse.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Child abuse" means an act that involves a minor victim that constitutes a violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.221">609.221</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.222">609.222</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.223">609.223</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.224">609.224</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2242">609.2242</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.282">609.282</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.322">609.322</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.324">609.324</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.343">609.343</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.344">609.344</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3458">609.3458</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.377">609.377</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.378">609.378</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.246">617.246</a>, or that is physical or sexual abuse as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.03">260E.03</a>, or an act committed in another state that involves a minor victim and would constitute a violation of one of these sections if committed in this state.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Child in need of protection or services.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Child in need of protection or services" means a child who is in need of protection or services because the child:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is abandoned or without parent, guardian, or custodian;</p>
          <p class="in">(2)(i) has been a victim of physical or sexual abuse as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.03#stat.260E.03.18">260E.03, subdivision 18</a> or 20, (ii) resides with or has resided with a victim of child abuse as defined in subdivision 5 or domestic child abuse as defined in subdivision 13, (iii) resides with or would reside with a perpetrator of domestic child abuse as defined in subdivision 13 or child abuse as defined in subdivision 5 or 13, or (iv) is a victim of emotional maltreatment as defined in subdivision 15;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) is without necessary food, clothing, shelter, education, or other required care for the child's physical or mental health or morals because the child's parent, guardian, or custodian is unable or unwilling to provide that care;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) is without the special care made necessary by a physical, mental, or emotional condition because the child's parent, guardian, or custodian is unable or unwilling to provide that care;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) is medically neglected, which includes, but is not limited to, the withholding of medically indicated treatment from an infant with a disability with a life-threatening condition. The term "withholding of medically indicated treatment" means the failure to respond to the infant's life-threatening conditions by providing treatment, including appropriate nutrition, hydration, and medication which, in the treating physician's, advanced practice registered nurse's, or physician assistant's reasonable medical judgment, will be most likely to be effective in ameliorating or correcting all conditions, except that the term does not include the failure to provide treatment other than appropriate nutrition, hydration, or medication to an infant when, in the treating physician's, advanced practice registered nurse's, or physician assistant's reasonable medical judgment:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) the infant is chronically and irreversibly comatose;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) the provision of the treatment would merely prolong dying, not be effective in ameliorating or correcting all of the infant's life-threatening conditions, or otherwise be futile in terms of the survival of the infant; or</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) the provision of the treatment would be virtually futile in terms of the survival of the infant and the treatment itself under the circumstances would be inhumane;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) is one whose parent, guardian, or other custodian for good cause desires to be relieved of the child's care and custody, including a child who entered foster care under a voluntary placement agreement between the parent and the responsible social services agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.227">260C.227</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) has been placed for adoption or care in violation of law;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) is without proper parental care because of the emotional, mental, or physical disability, or state of immaturity of the child's parent, guardian, or other custodian. A child is not considered to be without proper parental care based solely on the disability of the child's parent, guardian, or custodian;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) is one whose behavior, condition, or environment is such as to be injurious or dangerous to the child or others. An injurious or dangerous environment may include, but is not limited to, the exposure of a child to criminal activity in the child's home;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) is experiencing growth delays, which may be referred to as failure to thrive, that have been diagnosed by a physician and are due to parental neglect;</p>
          <p class="in">(11) is a sexually exploited youth;</p>
          <p class="in">(12) is a labor trafficked youth;</p>
          <p class="in">(13) has committed a delinquent act or a juvenile petty offense before becoming ten years old. This clause expires July 31, 2026;</p>
          <p class="in">(14) is a runaway;</p>
          <p class="in">(15) is a habitual truant;</p>
          <p class="in">(16) has been found incompetent to proceed or has been found not guilty by reason of mental illness or mental deficiency in connection with a delinquency proceeding, a certification under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260B.125">260B.125</a>, an extended jurisdiction juvenile prosecution, or a proceeding involving a juvenile petty offense;</p>
          <p>(17) has a parent whose parental rights to one or more other children were involuntarily terminated or whose custodial rights to another child have been involuntarily transferred to a relative and there is a case plan prepared by the responsible social services agency documenting a compelling reason why filing the termination of parental rights petition under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503#stat.260C.503.2">260C.503, subdivision 2</a>, is not in the best interests of the child; or</p>
          <p>(18) effective August 1, 2026, has committed a delinquent act or a juvenile petty offense before becoming 13 years old.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Child-placing agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Child-placing agency" means anyone licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.01">142B.01</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.79">142B.79</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/252.28#stat.252.28.2">252.28, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Compelling reasons.</span></h2>
          <p>"Compelling reasons" means an individualized determination by the responsible social services agency, which is approved by the court, related to a request by the agency not to initiate proceedings to terminate parental rights or transfer permanent legal and physical custody of a child to the child's relative or former noncustodial parent under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503#stat.260C.503.2">260C.503, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Court.</span></h2>
          <p>"Court" means juvenile court unless otherwise specified in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Custodian.</span></h2>
          <p>"Custodian" means any person who is under a legal obligation to provide care and support for a minor or who is in fact providing care and support for a minor. This subdivision does not impose upon persons who are not otherwise legally responsible for providing a child with necessary food, clothing, shelter, education, or medical care a duty to provide that care. For an Indian child, custodian means any Indian person who has legal custody of an Indian child under tribal law or custom or under state law or to whom temporary physical care, custody, and control has been transferred by the parent of the child, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.10">260.755, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Delinquent child.</span></h2>
          <p>"Delinquent child" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260B.007#stat.260B.007.6">260B.007, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Developmental disability.</span></h2>
          <p>"Developmental disability" means developmental disability as defined in United States Code, title 42, section 6001(8).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Domestic child abuse.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">"Domestic child abuse" means:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) any physical injury to a minor family or household member inflicted by an adult family or household member other than by accidental means;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) subjection of a minor family or household member by an adult family or household member to any act which constitutes a violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.321">609.321</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.324">609.324</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.343">609.343</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.344">609.344</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3458">609.3458</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.246">617.246</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) physical or sexual abuse as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.03">260E.03</a>, subdivision 18 or 20.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Egregious harm.</span></h2>
          <p>"Egregious harm" means the infliction of bodily harm to a child or neglect of a child which demonstrates a grossly inadequate ability to provide minimally adequate parental care. The egregious harm need not have occurred in the state or in the county where a termination of parental rights action has proper venue. Egregious harm includes, but is not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) conduct toward a child that constitutes a violation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.185">609.185</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.2114">609.2114</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.222#stat.609.222.2">609.222, subdivision 2</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.223">609.223</a>, or any other similar law of any other state;</p>
          <p>(2) the infliction of "substantial bodily harm" to a child, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.02#stat.609.02.7a">609.02, subdivision 7a</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) conduct toward a child that constitutes felony malicious punishment of a child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.377">609.377</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) conduct toward a child that constitutes felony unreasonable restraint of a child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.255#stat.609.255.3">609.255, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) conduct toward a child that constitutes felony neglect or endangerment of a child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.378">609.378</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) conduct toward a child that constitutes assault under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.221">609.221</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.222">609.222</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.223">609.223</a>;</p>
          <p>(7) conduct toward a child that constitutes sex trafficking, solicitation, inducement, promotion of, or receiving profit derived from prostitution under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.322">609.322</a>;</p>
          <p>(8) conduct toward a child that constitutes murder or voluntary manslaughter as defined by United States Code, title 18, section 1111(a) or 1112(a);</p>
          <p>(9) conduct toward a child that constitutes aiding or abetting, attempting, conspiring, or soliciting to commit a murder or voluntary manslaughter that constitutes a violation of United States Code, title 18, section 1111(a) or 1112(a); or</p>
          <p>(10) conduct toward a child that constitutes criminal sexual conduct under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a> or sexual extortion under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3458">609.3458</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Emotional maltreatment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emotional maltreatment" means the consistent, deliberate infliction of mental harm on a child by a person responsible for the child's care, that has an observable, sustained, and adverse effect on the child's physical, mental, or emotional development. "Emotional maltreatment" does not include reasonable training or discipline administered by the person responsible for the child's care or the reasonable exercise of authority by that person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Emotionally disturbed.</span></h2>
          <p>"Emotionally disturbed" means emotional disturbance as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.15">245.4871, subdivision 15</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.16a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16a.<span class="headnote">Family and permanency team.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family and permanency team" means a team consisting of the child's parent or legal custodian, relatives, foster care providers, and professionals who are resources to the child's family such as teachers, medical or mental health providers who have treated the child, or clergy, as appropriate. In the case of an Indian child, the family and permanency team includes tribal representatives, delegates, and cultural resources as identified by the child's tribe. Consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b), if the child is age 14 or older, the team must also include two team members that the child selects who are not the child's foster parent or caseworker. The responsible social services agency may reject an individual that the child selects if the agency has good cause to believe that the individual would not act in the best interests of the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.16b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16b.<span class="headnote">Family foster home.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family foster home" means the home of an individual or family who is licensed for child foster care under Minnesota Statutes, chapter 142B, meeting the standards in Minnesota Rules, chapter 2960, excluding foster residence settings licensed under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3000">2960.3000</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3200">2960.3200</a>, or licensed or approved by a tribe in accordance with tribal standards with whom the foster child resides. Family foster home includes an emergency unlicensed relative placement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.06">142B.06</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Family or household members.</span></h2>
          <p>"Family or household members" means spouses, former spouses, parents and children, persons related by blood, and persons who are presently residing together or who have resided together in the past, and persons who have a child in common regardless of whether they have been married or have lived together at any time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Foster care" means 24-hour substitute care for a child for whom a responsible social services agency has placement and care responsibility and:</p>
          <p>(1) who is placed away from the child's parent or guardian in foster family homes, foster homes of relatives, group homes, emergency shelters, residential facilities not excluded in this subdivision, child care institutions, and preadoptive homes;</p>
          <p>(2) who is colocated with the child's parent or guardian in a licensed residential family-based substance use disorder treatment program as defined in subdivision 22a; or</p>
          <p>(3) who is returned to the care of the child's parent or guardian from whom the child was removed under a trial home visit pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.1">260C.201, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (3).</p>
          <p>(b) A child is in foster care under this definition regardless of whether the facility is licensed and payments are made for the cost of care. Nothing in this definition creates any authority to place a child in a home or facility that is required to be licensed which is not licensed. "Foster care" does not include placement in any of the following facilities: hospitals, inpatient chemical dependency treatment facilities where the child is the recipient of the treatment, facilities that are primarily for delinquent children, any corrections facility or program within a particular correction's facility not meeting requirements for title IV-E facilities as determined by the commissioner, facilities to which a child is committed under the provision of chapter 253B, forestry camps, or jails. Foster care is intended to provide for a child's safety or to access treatment. Foster care must not be used as a punishment or consequence for a child's behavior.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Habitual truant.</span></h2>
          <p>"Habitual truant" means a child under the age of 17 years who is absent from attendance at school without lawful excuse for seven school days per school year if the child is in elementary school or for one or more class periods on seven school days per school year if the child is in middle school, junior high school, or high school or a child who is 17 years of age who is absent from attendance at school without lawful excuse for one or more class periods on seven school days per school year and who has not lawfully withdrawn from school under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22#stat.120A.22.8">120A.22, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Indian.</span></h2>
          <p>"Indian," consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.7">260.755, subdivision 7</a>, means a person who is a member of an Indian tribe or who is an Alaskan native and a member of a regional corporation as defined in section 7 of the Alaska Native Claims Settlement Act, United States Code, title 43, section 1606.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">Indian child.</span></h2>
          <p>"Indian child," consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.8">260.755, subdivision 8</a>, means an unmarried person who is under age 18 and is:</p>
          <p>(1) a member of an Indian tribe; or</p>
          <p>(2) eligible for membership in an Indian tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.21a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21a.<span class="headnote">Legal authority to place the child. </span></h2>
          <p>"Legal authority to place the child" means that the agency has legal responsibility for the care and control of the child while the child is in foster care. The agency may have legal authority to place a child through a court order under this chapter through a voluntary placement agreement between the agency and the child's parent under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.227">260C.227</a> or, in the case of an Indian child, through tribal court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Legal custody.</span></h2>
          <p>"Legal custody" means the right to the care, custody, and control of a child who has been taken from a parent by the court in accordance with the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201">260C.201</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.317">260C.317</a>. The expenses of legal custody are paid in accordance with the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.331">260C.331</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.22a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22a.<span class="headnote">Licensed residential family-based substance use disorder treatment program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Licensed residential family-based substance use disorder treatment program" means a residential treatment facility that provides the parent or guardian with parenting skills training, parent education, or individual and family counseling, under an organizational structure and treatment framework that involves understanding, recognizing, and responding to the effects of all types of trauma according to recognized principles of a trauma-informed approach and trauma-specific interventions to address the consequences of trauma and facilitate healing. The residential program must be licensed by the Department of Human Services under chapters 245A and 245G or Tribally licensed or approved as a residential substance use disorder treatment program specializing in the treatment of clients with children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Minor.</span></h2>
          <p>"Minor" means an individual under 18 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">Neglected and in foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>"Neglected and in foster care" means a child:</p>
          <p>(1) who has been placed in foster care by court order; and</p>
          <p>(2) whose parents' circumstances, condition, or conduct are such that the child cannot be returned to them; and</p>
          <p>(3) whose parents, despite the availability of needed rehabilitative services, have failed to make reasonable efforts to adjust their circumstances, condition or conduct, or have willfully failed to meet reasonable expectations with regard to visiting the child or providing financial support for the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Parent.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Parent" means a person who has a legal parent and child relationship with a child which confers or imposes on the person legal rights, privileges, duties, and obligations consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.51">257.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.74">257.74</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.75">257.75</a>. It includes the mother and child relationship and the father and child relationship. For matters governed by the Indian Child Welfare Act, parent includes any Indian person who has adopted a child by tribal law or custom, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.14">260.755, subdivision 14</a>, and does not include the unwed father where paternity has not been acknowledged or established.</p>
          <p>(b) A legally recognized parent and child relationship is established for purposes of this chapter between:</p>
          <p>(1) a child and a biological mother, by proof of her having given birth to the child, or under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.51">257.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.74">257.74</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.75">257.75</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) a child and father when:</p>
          <p>(i) there is a presumption of paternity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.55#stat.257.55.1">257.55, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), (b), or (c), and no action has been taken to declare the nonexistence of the father and child relationship;</p>
          <p>(ii) there is a presumption of paternity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.55#stat.257.55.1">257.55, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (d), and there is an adjudication of paternity under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.51">257.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.74">257.74</a>, or the father and mother have signed a recognition of parentage having the effect of an adjudication under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.75">257.75</a>;</p>
          <p>(iii) there is a presumption of paternity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.55#stat.257.55.1">257.55, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (e), (f), (g), or (h), and there is an adjudication of paternity under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.51">257.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.74">257.74</a>;</p>
          <p>(iv) there is no presumption of paternity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.55">257.55</a>, but the father has been adjudicated by court order under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.51">257.51</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.74">257.74</a>;</p>
          <p>(v) there is no presumption of paternity under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.55">257.55</a>, but the father and mother have signed a recognition of parentage having the effect of adjudication under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.75">257.75</a>;</p>
          <p>(vi) there is a positive test result under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.62#stat.257.62.5">257.62, subdivision 5</a>, and the father is adjudicated as the father of the child either by court order under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.51">257.51 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.74">257.74</a>, or because the father and the child's mother have signed a recognition of parentage having the effect of adjudication under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/257.75">257.75</a>; or</p>
          <p>(vii) the parent and child relationship is established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755">260.755</a>, subdivision 14; or</p>
          <p>(3) a child and an adoptive parent by proof of adoption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.25a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25a.<span class="headnote">Permanency plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Permanency plan" means the established goal in the out-of-home placement plan that will achieve a safe, permanent home for the child. There are four permanency goals for children:</p>
          <p>(1) reunification with the child's parent or legal guardian;</p>
          <p>(2) placement with other relatives;</p>
          <p>(3) adoption; or</p>
          <p>(4) establishment of a new legal guardianship.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Person.</span></h2>
          <p>"Person" includes any individual, association, corporation, partnership, and the state or any of its political subdivisions, departments, or agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.26a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26a.<span class="headnote">Putative father.</span></h2>
          <p>"Putative father" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.21#stat.259.21.12">259.21, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.26b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26b.<span class="headnote">Relative of an Indian child.</span></h2>
          <p>"Relative of an Indian child" means a person who is a member of the Indian child's family as defined in the Indian Child Welfare Act of 1978, United States Code, title 25, section 1903, paragraphs (2), (6), and (9), and who is an extended family member as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.5b">260.755, subdivision 5b</a>, of the Minnesota Indian Family Preservation Act.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.26c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26c.<span class="headnote">Qualified individual.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Qualified individual" means a trained culturally competent professional or licensed clinician, including a mental health professional under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.27">245.4871, subdivision 27</a>, who is qualified to conduct the assessment approved by the commissioner. The qualified individual must not be an employee of the responsible social services agency or an individual connected to or affiliated with any placement setting in which a responsible social services agency has placed children.</p>
          <p>(b) When the Indian Child Welfare Act of 1978, United States Code, title 25, sections 1901 to 1963, applies to a child, the county must contact the child's tribe without delay to give the tribe the option to designate a qualified individual who is a trained culturally competent professional or licensed clinician, including a mental health professional under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.27">245.4871, subdivision 27</a>, who is not employed by the responsible social services agency and who is not connected to or affiliated with any placement setting in which a responsible social services agency has placed children. Only a federal waiver that demonstrates maintained objectivity may allow a responsible social services agency employee or Tribal employee affiliated with any placement setting in which the responsible social services agency has placed children to be designated the qualified individual.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.26d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26d.<span class="headnote">Qualified residential treatment program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Qualified residential treatment program" means a children's residential treatment program licensed under chapter 245A or licensed or approved by a tribe that is approved to receive foster care maintenance payments under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.418">142A.418</a> that:</p>
          <p>(1) has a trauma-informed treatment model designed to address the needs of children with serious emotional or behavioral disorders or disturbances;</p>
          <p>(2) has registered or licensed nursing staff and other licensed clinical staff who:</p>
          <p>(i) provide care within the scope of their practice; and</p>
          <p>(ii) are available 24 hours per day and seven days per week;</p>
          <p>(3) is accredited by any of the following independent, nonprofit organizations: the Commission on Accreditation of Rehabilitation Facilities (CARF), the Joint Commission on Accreditation of Healthcare Organizations (JCAHO), and the Council on Accreditation (COA), or any other nonprofit accrediting organization approved by the United States Department of Health and Human Services;</p>
          <p>(4) if it is in the child's best interests, facilitates participation of the child's family members in the child's treatment programming consistent with the child's out-of-home placement plan under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) facilitates outreach to family members of the child, including siblings;</p>
          <p>(6) documents how the facility facilitates outreach to the child's parents and relatives, as well as documents the child's parents' and other relatives' contact information;</p>
          <p>(7) documents how the facility includes family members in the child's treatment process, including after the child's discharge, and how the facility maintains the child's sibling connections; and</p>
          <p>(8) provides the child and child's family with discharge planning and family-based aftercare support for at least six months after the child's discharge. Aftercare support may include clinical care consultation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0671#stat.256B.0671.7">256B.0671, subdivision 7</a>, and mental health certified family peer specialist services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0616">256B.0616</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">Relative.</span></h2>
          <p>"Relative" means a person related to the child by blood, marriage, or adoption; the legal parent, guardian, or custodian of the child's siblings; or an individual who is an important friend of the child or of the child's parent or custodian, including an individual with whom the child has resided or had significant contact or who has a significant relationship to the child or the child's parent or custodian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.27a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27a.<span class="headnote">Responsible social services agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Responsible social services agency" means the county social services agency that has responsibility for public child welfare and child protection services and includes the provision of adoption services as an agent of the commissioner of children, youth, and families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.27b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27b.<span class="headnote">Residential treatment facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Residential treatment facility" means a 24-hour-a-day program that provides treatment for children with emotional disturbance, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4871#stat.245.4871.32">245.4871, subdivision 32</a>, and includes a licensed residential program specializing in caring 24 hours a day for children with a developmental delay or related condition. A residential treatment facility does not include a psychiatric residential treatment facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0941">256B.0941</a> or a family foster home as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 16b.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Runaway.</span></h2>
          <p>"Runaway" means an unmarried child under the age of 18 years who is absent from the home of a parent or other lawful placement without the consent of the parent, guardian, or lawful custodian.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Secure detention facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Secure detention facility" means a physically restricting facility, including but not limited to a jail, a hospital, a state institution, a residential treatment center, or a detention home used for the temporary care of a child pending court action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Shelter care facility.</span></h2>
          <p>"Shelter care facility" means a physically unrestricting facility, such as but not limited to, a hospital, a group home or a licensed facility for foster care, used for the temporary care of a child pending court action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.31">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 31.<span class="headnote">Sexually exploited youth.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sexually exploited youth" means an individual who:</p>
          <p>(1) is alleged to have engaged in conduct which would, if committed by an adult, violate any federal, state, or local law relating to being hired, offering to be hired, or agreeing to be hired by another individual to engage in sexual penetration or sexual conduct;</p>
          <p>(2) is a victim of a crime described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.343">609.343</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.344">609.344</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3451">609.3451</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3453">609.3453</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3458">609.3458</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.352">609.352</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.246">617.246</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.247">617.247</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) is a victim of a crime described in United States Code, title 18, section 2260; 2421; 2422; 2423; 2425; 2425A; or 2256;</p>
          <p>(4) is a sex trafficking victim as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.321#stat.609.321.7b">609.321, subdivision 7b</a>; or</p>
          <p>(5) is a victim of commercial sexual exploitation as defined in United States Code, title 22, section 7102(11)(A) and (12).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.32">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 32.<span class="headnote">Sibling.</span></h2>
          <p>"Sibling" means one of two or more individuals who have one or both parents in common through blood, marriage, or adoption. This includes siblings as defined by the child's tribal code or custom. Sibling also includes an individual who would have been considered a sibling but for a termination of parental rights of one or both parents, suspension of parental rights under tribal code, or other disruption of parental rights such as the death of a parent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.33">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 33.<span class="headnote">Labor trafficked youth. </span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "labor trafficked youth" means a child, as defined in subdivision 4, who:</p>
          <p>(1) is a labor trafficking victim as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.281#stat.609.281.6">609.281, subdivision 6</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) is a victim of severe forms of trafficking in persons as defined in United States Code, title 22, section 7102(11)(B).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.007.34">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 34.<span class="headnote">Human trafficking.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "human trafficking" includes labor trafficking as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.281#stat.609.281.5">609.281, subdivision 5</a>; sex trafficking as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.321#stat.609.321.7a">609.321, subdivision 7a</a>; and severe forms of trafficking in persons as defined in United States Code, title 22, section 7102(11).</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.007.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.2.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 2</a>; art 4 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.8.43.0">1999 c 245 art 8 s 43</a>-45; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/1/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp2011 c 1 art 4 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.3.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 3-6; art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.39.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 39</a>,40;       <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendments to subdivisions 5 and 6 by Laws 2024, chapter 115, article 12, sections 6 and 7, are effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2024, chapter 115, article 12, sections 6 and 7, the effective dates.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.008">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.008 FOSTER CARE SIBLING BILL OF RIGHTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.008.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Statement of rights.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A child placed in foster care who has a sibling has the right to:</p>
          <p>(1) be placed in foster care homes with the child's siblings, when possible and when it is in the best interest of each sibling, in order to sustain family relationships;</p>
          <p>(2) be placed in close geographical distance to the child's siblings, if placement together is not possible, to facilitate frequent and meaningful contact;</p>
          <p>(3) have frequent contact with the child's siblings in foster care and, whenever possible, with the child's siblings who are not in foster care, unless the responsible social services agency has documented that contact is not in the best interest of any sibling. Contact includes but is not limited to telephone calls, text messaging, social media and other Internet use, and video calls;</p>
          <p>(4) annually receive a telephone number, address, and email address for all siblings in foster care, and receive updated photographs of siblings regularly, by regular mail or email;</p>
          <p>(5) participate in regular face-to-face visits with the child's siblings in foster care and, whenever possible, with the child's siblings who are not in foster care. Participation in these visits shall not be withheld or restricted as a consequence for behavior, and shall only be restricted if the responsible social services agency documents that the visits are contrary to the safety or well-being of any sibling. Social workers, parents, foster care providers, and older children must cooperate to ensure regular visits and must coordinate dates, times, transportation, and other accommodations as necessary. The timing and regularity of visits shall be outlined in each sibling's service plan, based on the individual circumstances and needs of each child. A social worker need not give explicit permission for each visit or possible overnight visit, but foster care providers shall communicate with social workers about these visits;</p>
          <p>(6) be actively involved in each other's lives and share celebrations, if they choose to do so, including but not limited to birthdays, holidays, graduations, school and extracurricular activities, cultural customs in the siblings' native language, and other milestones;</p>
          <p>(7) be promptly informed about changes in sibling placements or circumstances, including but not limited to new placements, discharge from placements, significant life events, and discharge from foster care;</p>
          <p>(8) be included in permanency planning decisions for siblings, if appropriate; and</p>
          <p>(9) be informed of the expectations for and possibility of continued contact with a sibling after an adoption or transfer of permanent physical and legal custody to a relative.</p>
          <p>(b) Adult siblings of children in foster care shall have the right to be considered as foster care providers, adoptive parents, and relative custodians for their siblings, if they choose to do so.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.008.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Interpretation.</span></h2>
          <p>The rights under this section are established for the benefit of siblings in foster care. This statement of rights does not replace or diminish other rights, liberties, and responsibilities that may exist relative to children in foster care, adult siblings of children in foster care, foster care providers, parents, relatives, or responsible social services agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.008.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Disclosure.</span></h2>
          <p>Child welfare agency staff shall provide a copy of these rights to a child who has a sibling at the time the child enters foster care, to any adult siblings of a child entering foster care, if known, and to the foster care provider, in a format specified by the commissioner of children, youth, and families. The copy shall contain the address and telephone number of the Office of Ombudsperson for Families and a brief statement describing how to file a complaint with the office.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.008.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/188/#laws.0.3.0">2018 c 188 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EXPERT ASSISTANCE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.050">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.050 EXPERT ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <p>In any county the court may provide for the physical and mental diagnosis of cases of minors who are believed to have a physical disability, mental illness, or developmental disability, and for such purpose may appoint professionally qualified persons, whose compensation shall be fixed by the judge with the approval of the county board.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.050.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.3.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.121.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 121</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">JURISDICTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.101">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.101 JURISDICTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.101.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Children in need of protection or services, or neglected and in foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>The juvenile court has original and exclusive jurisdiction in proceedings concerning any child who is alleged to be in need of protection or services, or neglected and in foster care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.101.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Other matters relating to children.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> The juvenile court has original and exclusive jurisdiction in proceedings concerning:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the termination of parental rights to a child in accordance with the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301">260C.301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.328">260C.328</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) permanency matters under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) the appointment and removal of a juvenile court guardian for a child, where parental rights have been terminated under the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301">260C.301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.328">260C.328</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) judicial consent to the marriage of a child when required by law;</p>
          <p>(5) all adoption matters and review of the efforts to finalize the adoption of the child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.317">260C.317</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) the review of the placement of a child who is in foster care pursuant to a voluntary placement agreement between the child's parent or parents and the responsible social services agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.227">260C.227</a>; or between the child, when the child is over age 18, and the agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.229">260C.229</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) the review of voluntary foster care placement of a child for treatment under chapter 260D according to the review requirements of that chapter;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) the reestablishment of a legal parent and child relationship under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.329">260C.329</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(9) juvenile court guardianship petitions for at-risk juveniles filed under chapter 257D.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.101.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Matters relating to domestic child abuse.</span></h2>
          <p>The juvenile court has jurisdiction in proceedings concerning any alleged acts of domestic child abuse. In a jurisdiction which utilizes referees in child in need of protection or services matters, the court or judge may refer actions under this subdivision to a referee to take and report the evidence in the action. If the respondent does not appear after service is duly made and proved, the court may hear and determine the proceeding as a default matter. Proceedings under this subdivision shall be given docket priority by the court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.101.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Parents and guardians.</span></h2>
          <p>A parent, guardian, or custodian of a child who is subject to the jurisdiction of the court is also subject to the jurisdiction of the court in any matter in which that parent, guardian, or custodian has a right to notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.151">260C.151</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.152">260C.152</a>, or the right to participate under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163">260C.163</a>. In any proceeding concerning a child alleged to be in need of protection or services, the court has jurisdiction over a parent, guardian, or custodian for the purposes of a disposition order issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.6">260C.201, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.101.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Indian children.</span></h2>
          <p>In a child in need of protection or services proceeding, when an Indian child is a ward of a tribal court with federally recognized child welfare jurisdiction, the Indian tribe retains exclusive jurisdiction notwithstanding the residence or domicile of an Indian child, as provided in the Indian Child Welfare Act of 1978, United States Code, title 25, section 1911.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.4.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 4</a>; art 4 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.4.16.0">2005 c 10 art 4 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/361/#laws.6.28.0">2008 c 361 art 6 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.7.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/30/#laws.0.1.0">2013 c 30 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/45/#laws.0.13.0">2022 c 45 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.121">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.121 VENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.121.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Venue.</span></h2>
          <p>When it is alleged that a child is in need of protection or services, venue may be in the county where the child is found, in the county of residence, or in the county where the alleged conditions causing the child's need for protection or services occurred.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.121.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>The judge of the juvenile court may transfer any proceedings brought under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.101">260C.101</a>, except adoptions, to the juvenile court of a county having venue as provided in subdivision 1, at any stage of the proceedings and in the following manner. When it appears that the best interests of the child, society, or the convenience of proceedings will be served by a transfer, the court may transfer the case to the juvenile court of the county of the child's residence. With the consent of the receiving court, the court may also transfer the case to the juvenile court of the county where the child is found. The court transfers the case by ordering a continuance and by forwarding to the court administrator of the appropriate juvenile court a certified copy of all papers filed, together with an order of transfer. The judge of the receiving court may accept the findings of the transferring court or may direct the filing of a new petition or notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.143">260C.143</a> and hear the case anew.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.121.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Resident of another state.</span></h2>
          <p>If it appears at any stage of the proceeding that a child before the court is a resident of another state, the court may invoke the provisions of the interstate compact on juveniles or, if it is in the best interests of the child or the public to do so, the court may place the child in the custody of the child's parent, guardian, or custodian, if the parent, guardian, or custodian agrees to accept custody of the child and return the child to their state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.5.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.125">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.125 CASE TRANSFER PROCESS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.125.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>This section pertains to the transfer of responsibility for the placement and care of an Indian child in out-of-home placement from the responsible social services agency to a tribal title IV-E agency or an Indian tribe in and outside of Minnesota with a title IV-E agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.125.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment of transfer procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The responsible social services agency shall establish and maintain procedures, in consultation with Indian tribes, for the transfer of responsibility for placement and care of a child to a tribal agency. Transfer of a child's case under this section shall not affect the child's title IV-E and Medicaid eligibility.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.125.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Title IV-E eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>If a child's title IV-E eligibility has not been determined by the responsible social services agency by the time of transfer, it shall be established at the time of the transfer by the responsible social services agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.125.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Documentation and information.</span></h2>
          <p>Essential documents and information shall be transferred to a tribal agency, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) district court judicial determinations to the effect that continuation in the home from which the child was removed would be contrary to the welfare of the child and that reasonable efforts were made to ensure placement prevention and family reunification pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.012">260.012</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) documentation related to the child's permanency proceeding under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) documentation from the responsible social services agency related to the child's title IV-E eligibility;</p>
          <p>(4) documentation regarding the child's eligibility or potential eligibility for other federal benefits;</p>
          <p>(5) the child's case plan, developed pursuant to the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, sections 675(1) and 675a, including health and education records of the child pursuant to the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, section 675(1)(c); and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, and information; and</p>
          <p>(6) documentation of the child's placement setting, including a copy of the most recent provider's license.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.125.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.15.6.0">2016 c 189 art 15 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">NEWBORN SAFE PLACES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.139">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.139 GIVE LIFE A CHANCE; SAFE PLACE FOR NEWBORNS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.139.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duty to attempt reunification, duty to search for relatives, and preferences not applicable.</span></h2>
          <p>A responsible social service agency with responsibility for a child pursuant to subdivision 5 is not required to attempt to reunify the child with the child's parents. Additionally, the agency is not required to search for relatives of the child as a placement or permanency option under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>, or to implement other placement requirements that give a preference to relatives if the agency does not have information as to the identity of the child, the child's mother, or the child's father.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.139.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "safe place" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.902">145.902</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.139.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Status of child.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of proceedings under this chapter and adoption proceedings, a newborn left at a safe place, pursuant to subdivision 4 and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.902">145.902</a>, is considered an abandoned child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.03#stat.260E.03.22">260E.03, subdivision 22</a>, clause (2). The child is abandoned under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 6, clause (1), and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301#stat.260C.301.1">260C.301, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), clause (1).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.139.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Relinquishment of a newborn.</span></h2>
          <p>A mother or any person, with the mother's permission, may bring a newborn infant to a safe place during its hours of operation and leave the infant in the care of an employee of the safe place. The mother or a person with the mother's permission may call 911 to request to have an ambulance dispatched to an agreed-upon location to relinquish a newborn infant into the custody of ambulance personnel.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.139.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Placement of newborn.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency contacted by a safe place pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.902#stat.145.902.2">145.902, subdivision 2</a>, shall have legal responsibility for the placement of the newborn infant in foster care for 72 hours during which time the agency shall file a petition under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.141">260C.141</a> and ask the court to order continued placement of the child in foster care. The agency shall immediately begin planning for adoptive placement of the newborn.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.139.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/421/#laws.0.2.0">2000 c 421 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.2.2.0">2012 c 216 art 2 s 2; art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.126.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 126</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.86.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 86</a>,87; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.117.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 117</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PROCEDURES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.141">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.141 PETITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.141.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Who may file; required form.</span></h2>
