          <p class="in">(d) If the responsible social services agency determines that reimbursement is in the child's best interests, the court shall order the amount of reimbursement attributable to the parents or custodian, or attributable to the child, or attributable to both sources, withheld under chapter 518A from the income of the parents or the custodian of the child. A parent or custodian who fails to pay without good reason may be proceeded against for contempt, or the court may inform the county attorney, who shall proceed to collect the unpaid sums, or both procedures may be used.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) If the court orders a physical or mental examination for a child, the examination is a medically necessary service for purposes of determining whether the service is covered by a health insurance policy, health maintenance contract, or other health coverage plan. Court-ordered treatment shall be subject to policy, contract, or plan requirements for medical necessity. Nothing in this paragraph changes or eliminates benefit limits, conditions of coverage, co-payments or deductibles, provider restrictions, or other requirements in the policy, contract, or plan that relate to coverage of other medically necessary services.</p>
          <p>(f) Notwithstanding paragraph (b), (c), or (d), a parent, custodian, or guardian of the child is not required to use income and resources attributable to the child to reimburse the county for costs of care and is not required to contribute to the cost of care of the child during any period of time when the child is returned to the home of that parent, custodian, or guardian pursuant to a trial home visit under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.1">260C.201, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.331.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cost of group foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever a child is placed in a group foster care facility as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.1">260C.201, subdivision 1, paragraph (b), clause (2) or (3)</a>, the cost of providing the care shall, upon certification by the juvenile court, be paid from the welfare fund of the county in which the proceedings were held. To reimburse the counties for the costs of promoting the establishment of suitable group foster homes, the state shall quarterly, from funds appropriated for that purpose, reimburse counties 50 percent of the costs not paid by federal and other available state aids and grants. Reimbursement shall be prorated if the appropriation is insufficient.</p>
          <p>The commissioner of corrections shall establish procedures for reimbursement and certify to the commissioner of management and budget each county entitled to receive state aid under the provisions of this subdivision. Upon receipt of a certificate the commissioner of management and budget shall issue a state payment to the county treasurer for the amount due, together with a copy of the certificate prepared by the commissioner of corrections.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.331.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Court expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The following expenses are a charge upon the county in which proceedings are held upon certification of the judge of juvenile court or upon such other authorization provided by law:</p>
          <p>(1) the fees and mileage of witnesses, and the expenses and mileage of officers serving notices and subpoenas ordered by the court, as prescribed by law;</p>
          <p>(2) the expense of transporting a child to a place designated by a child-placing agency for the care of the child if the court transfers legal custody to a child-placing agency;</p>
          <p>(3) the expense of transporting a minor to a place designated by the court;</p>
          <p>(4) reasonable compensation for an attorney appointed by the court to serve as counsel.</p>
          <p>The State Guardian Ad Litem Board shall pay for guardian ad litem expenses and reasonable compensation for an attorney to serve as counsel for a guardian ad litem, if necessary. In no event may the court order that guardian ad litem expenses or compensation for an attorney serving as counsel for a guardian ad litem be charged to a county.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.331.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Legal settlement.</span></h2>
          <p>The county charged with the costs and expenses under subdivisions 1 and 3 may recover these costs and expenses from the county where the minor has legal settlement for general assistance purposes by filing verified claims which shall be payable as are other claims against the county. A detailed statement of the facts upon which the claim is based shall accompany the claim. If a dispute relating to general assistance settlement arises, the responsible social services agency of the county denying legal settlement shall send a detailed statement of the facts upon which the claim is denied together with a copy of the detailed statement of the facts upon which the claim is based to the commissioner of human services. The commissioner shall immediately investigate and determine the question of general assistance settlement and shall certify findings to the responsible social services agency of each county. The decision of the commissioner is final and shall be complied with unless, within 30 days thereafter, action is taken in district court as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.20">142A.20</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.331.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Attorney fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In proceedings in which the court has appointed counsel pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163#stat.260C.163.3">260C.163, subdivision 3</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/611.14">611.14</a>, clause (4), for a minor unable to employ counsel, the court shall inquire into the ability of the parents to pay for such counsel's services and, after giving the parents a reasonable opportunity to be heard, may order the parents to pay attorney fees.</p>
          <p>(b) In proceedings in which the court has appointed counsel pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163#stat.260C.163.3">260C.163, subdivision 3</a>, for a parent, guardian, or custodian, the court shall inquire into the ability of the parents, guardians, or custodians to pay for such counsel's services and, after giving these persons a reasonable opportunity to be heard, may order the appropriate person to pay attorney fees.</p>
          <p>(c) The court may order the appropriate person or persons under paragraph (a) or (b), or both, to reimburse the governmental unit providing counsel for the cost of appointed counsel. In determining the amount of reimbursement, the court shall consider the appropriate person's income, assets, and employment. If reimbursement is required under this subdivision, the court shall order the reimbursement when counsel is first appointed or as soon as possible after the court determines that reimbursement is required. The court may accept partial reimbursement from a person if the person's financial circumstances warrant establishing a reduced reimbursement schedule. If the person does not agree to make payments, the court may order the person's employer to withhold a percentage of the person's income to be turned over to the court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.331.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Guardian ad litem fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In proceedings in which the court appoints a guardian ad litem pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163#stat.260C.163.5">260C.163, subdivision 5, paragraph (a)</a>, the court may inquire into the ability of the parents to pay for the guardian ad litem's services and, after giving the parents a reasonable opportunity to be heard, may order the parents to pay guardian ad litem fees.</p>
          <p>(b) In each fiscal year, the commissioner of management and budget shall deposit guardian ad litem reimbursements in the special revenue fund and credit them to a separate account with the State Guardian Ad Litem Board. The balance of this account is appropriated to the State Guardian Ad Litem Board and does not cancel but is available until expended. Revenue from this account must be spent in the judicial district in which the reimbursement is collected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.331.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the responsible social services agency receives Retirement, Survivors, and Disability Insurance; Supplemental Security Income; veteran's benefits; railroad retirement benefits; or black lung benefits on behalf of a child, it must provide written notice by certified mail, return receipt requested to:</p>
          <p>(1) the child, if the child is 13 years of age or older;</p>
          <p>(2) the child's parent, guardian, or custodian or if there is no legal parent or custodian, the child's relative selected by the agency;</p>
          <p>(3) the guardian ad litem;</p>
          <p>(4) the legally responsible agency as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.602">142A.602</a>, if different than the responsible social services agency; and</p>
          <p>(5) the counsel appointed for the child pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163#stat.260C.163.3">260C.163, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) If the responsible social services agency receives benefits under this subdivision on behalf of a child 13 years of age or older, the legally responsible agency as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.602#stat.142A.602.13">142A.602, subdivision 13</a>, if different, and the guardian ad litem must disclose this information to the child in person in a manner that best helps the child understand the information. This paragraph does not apply in circumstances where the child is living outside of Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(c) If the responsible social services agency receives the benefits listed under this subdivision on behalf of a child, it cannot use those funds for any other purpose than the care of that child. The responsible social services agency must not commingle any benefits received under this subdivision and must not put the benefits received on behalf of a child into a general fund.</p>
          <p>(d) If the responsible social services agency receives any benefits listed under this subdivision, it must keep a record of:</p>
          <p>(1) the total dollar amount it received on behalf of all children it receives benefits for;</p>
          <p>(2) the total number of children it applied to be a payee for; and</p>
          <p>(3) the total number of children it receives benefits for. By July 1, 2025, and each July 1 thereafter, the responsible social services agency must submit a report to the commissioner that includes the information required under this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.331.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.36.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 36</a>; art 4 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/216/#laws.7.22.0">1999 c 216 art 7 s 22</a>,23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/178/#laws.1.44.0">2001 c 178 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/164/#laws.0.29.0">2005 c 164 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/7/#laws.0.28.0">1Sp2005 c 7 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.1.23.0">2007 c 147 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/150/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 150 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/269/#laws.2.2.0">2010 c 269 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/309/#laws.0.3.0">2010 c 309 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/1/1/#laws.14.10.0">1Sp2010 c 1 art 14 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/1/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp2011 c 1 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/212/#laws.0.6.0">2012 c 212 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.6.13.0">2012 c 216 art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/37/#laws.0.4.0">2013 c 37 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.62.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 62</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.40.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.8.22.0">2022 c 98 art 8 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.12.11.0">2024 c 115 art 12 s 11</a>; art 16 s 34</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.335">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.335 CIVIL JURISDICTION OVER PERSONS CONTRIBUTING TO NEED FOR PROTECTION OR SERVICES; COURT ORDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.335.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Jurisdiction.</span></h2>
          <p>The juvenile court has civil jurisdiction over persons contributing to the need for protection or services of a child under the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.335.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Petition; order to show cause.</span></h2>
          <p>A request for jurisdiction over a person described in subdivision 1 shall be initiated by the filing of a verified petition by the county attorney having jurisdiction over the place where the child is found, resides, or where the alleged act of contributing occurred. A prior or pending petition alleging that the child is in need of protection or services is not a prerequisite to a petition under this section. The petition shall allege the factual basis for the claim that the person is contributing to the child's need for protection or services. If the court determines, upon review of the verified petition, that probable cause exists to believe that the person has contributed to the child's need for protection or services, the court shall issue an order to show cause why the person should not be subject to the jurisdiction of the court. The order to show cause and a copy of the verified petition shall be served personally upon the person and shall set forth the time and place of the hearing to be conducted under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.335.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court shall conduct a hearing on the petition in accordance with the procedures contained in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) Hearings under this subdivision shall be without a jury. The rules of evidence promulgated pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/480.0591">480.0591</a> and the provisions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.165">260C.165</a> shall apply. In all proceedings under this section, the court shall admit only evidence that would be admissible in a civil trial. When the respondent is an adult, hearings under this subdivision shall be open to the public. Hearings shall be conducted within five days of personal service of the order to show cause and may be continued for a reasonable period of time if a continuance is in the best interest of the child or in the interests of justice.</p>
          <p>(c) At the conclusion of the hearing, if the court finds by a fair preponderance of the evidence that the person has contributed to the child's need for protection or services, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.425">260C.425</a>, the court may make any of the following orders:</p>
          <p>(1) restrain the person from any further act or omission in violation of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.425">260C.425</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) prohibit the person from associating or communicating in any manner with the child;</p>
          <p>(3) require the person to participate in evaluation or services determined necessary by the court to correct the conditions that contributed to the child's need for protection or services;</p>
          <p>(4) require the person to provide supervision, treatment, or other necessary care;</p>
          <p>(5) require the person to pay restitution to a victim for pecuniary damages arising from an act of the child relating to the child's need for protection or services;</p>
          <p>(6) require the person to pay the cost of services provided to the child or for the child's protection; or</p>
          <p>(7) require the person to provide for the child's maintenance or care if the person is responsible for the maintenance or care, and direct when, how, and where money for the maintenance or care shall be paid. If the person is receiving public assistance for the child's maintenance or care, the court shall authorize the public agency responsible for administering the public assistance funds to make payments directly to vendors for the cost of food, shelter, medical care, utilities, and other necessary expenses.</p>
          <p>(d) An order issued under this section shall be for a fixed period of time, not to exceed one year. The order may be renewed or modified prior to expiration upon notice and motion when there has not been compliance with the court's order or the order continues to be necessary to eliminate the contributing behavior or to mitigate its effect on the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.335.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Criminal proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>The county attorney may bring both a criminal proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.425">260C.425</a> and a civil action under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.335.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.37.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 37</a>; art 4 s 2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.401"><b>260C.401</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/260/#laws.0.97.0">2000 c 260 s 97</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.405">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.405 VIOLATION OF AN ORDER FOR PROTECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.405.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Violation; penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever an order for protection is granted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.148">260C.148</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.3">260C.201, subdivision 3</a>, restraining the person or excluding the person from the residence, and the respondent or person to be restrained knows of the order, violation of the order for protection is a misdemeanor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.405.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Arrest.</span></h2>
          <p>A peace officer shall arrest without a warrant and take into custody a person whom the peace officer has probable cause to believe has violated an order granted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.148">260C.148</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.3">260C.201, subdivision 3</a>, restraining the person or excluding the person from the residence, if the existence of the order can be verified by the officer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.405.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contempt.</span></h2>
          <p>A violation of an order for protection shall also constitute contempt of court and the person violating the order shall be subject to the penalties for contempt.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.405.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Order to show cause.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon the filing of an affidavit by the agency or any peace officer, alleging that the respondent has violated an order for protection granted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.148">260C.148</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.3">260C.201, subdivision 3</a>, the court may issue an order to the respondent, requiring the respondent to appear and show cause within 14 days why the respondent should not be found in contempt of court. The hearing may be held by the court in any county in which the child or respondent temporarily or permanently resides at the time of the alleged violation.</p>
          <p>A peace officer is not liable under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.43">609.43</a>, clause (1), for failure to perform a duty required by subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.405.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.39.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 39</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.411">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.411 NEW EVIDENCE.</h1>
        <p>A child whose status has been adjudicated by a juvenile court, or the child's parent, guardian, custodian or spouse may, at any time within 15 days of the filing of the court's order, petition the court for a rehearing on the ground that new evidence has been discovered affecting the advisability of the court's original adjudication or disposition. Upon a showing that such evidence does exist the court shall order that a new hearing be held within 30 days, unless the court extends this time period for good cause shown within the 30-day period, and shall make such disposition of the case as the facts and the best interests of the child warrant.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.40.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 40</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.415">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.415 APPEAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Persons entitled to appeal; procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>An appeal may be taken by the aggrieved person from a final order of the juvenile court affecting a substantial right of the aggrieved person, including but not limited to an order adjudging a child to be in need of protection or services, neglected and in foster care. The appeal shall be taken within 30 days of the filing of the appealable order. The court administrator shall notify the person having legal custody of the minor of the appeal. Failure to notify the person having legal custody of the minor shall not affect the jurisdiction of the appellate court. The order of the juvenile court shall stand, pending the determination of the appeal, but the reviewing court may in its discretion and upon application stay the order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>The appeal from a juvenile court is taken to the court of appeals as in civil cases, except as provided in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.41.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 41</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.419">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.419 STATEWIDE OFFICE OF APPELLATE COUNSEL AND TRAINING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As used in this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Board" means the State Board of Appellate Counsel and Training.</p>
          <p>(c) "Juvenile protection matter" means any of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) child in need of protection or services matters as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 6, including habitual truant and runaway matters;</p>
          <p>(2) neglected and in foster care matters as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.24">260C.007, subdivision 24</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) review of voluntary foster care matters as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.141">260C.141, subdivision </a>2;</p>
          <p>(4) review of out-of-home placement matters as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) termination of parental rights matters as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301">260C.301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.328">260C.328</a>; and</p>
          <p>(6) permanent placement matters as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>, including matters involving termination of parental rights, guardianship to the commissioner of children, youth, and families, transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative, permanent custody to the agency, temporary legal custody to the agency, and matters involving voluntary placement pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260D.07">260D.07</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Office" means the Statewide Office of Appellate Counsel and Training.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Statewide Office of Appellate Counsel and Training; establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Statewide Office of Appellate Counsel and Training is established as an independent state office. The office shall be responsible for:</p>
          <p>(1) establishing and maintaining a system for providing appellate representation to parents in juvenile protection matters, as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163#stat.260C.163.3">260C.163, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c), and in Tribal court jurisdictions;</p>
          <p>(2) providing training to all parent attorneys practicing in the state on topics relevant to their practice and establishing practice standards and training requirements for parent attorneys practicing in the state; and</p>
          <p>(3) collaborating with the Minnesota Department of Children, Youth, and Families to coordinate and secure federal Title IV-E support for counties and Tribes interested in accessing federal funding.</p>
          <p>(b) The office shall be governed by a board as provided in subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">State Board of Appellate Counsel and Training; structure; membership.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The State Board of Appellate Counsel and Training is established to direct the Statewide Office of Appellate Counsel and Training. The board shall consist of seven members, including:</p>
          <p>(1) four public members appointed by the governor; and</p>
          <p>(2) three members appointed by the supreme court, at least one of whom must have experience representing parents in juvenile court and who include two attorneys admitted to practice law in the state and one public member.</p>
          <p>(b) The appointing authorities may not appoint any of the following to be a member of the board:</p>
          <p>(1) a person who is a judge;</p>
          <p>(2) a person who is a registered lobbyist;</p>
          <p>(3) a person serving as a guardian ad litem or counsel for a guardian ad litem;</p>
          <p>(4) a person who serves as counsel for children in juvenile court;</p>
          <p>(5) a person under contract with or employed by the Department of Children, Youth, and Families or a county department of human or social services; or</p>
          <p>(6) a current city or county attorney or assistant city or county attorney.</p>
          <p>(c) All members shall demonstrate an interest in maintaining a high quality, independent appellate defense system for parents in juvenile protection proceedings who are unable to obtain adequate representation, a robust program for parent attorneys in Minnesota, and an efficient coordination effort, in collaboration with the Department of Children, Youth, and Families, to secure and utilize Title IV-E funding. At least one member of the board appointed by the governor must be a representative from a federally recognized Indian Tribe. No more than five members of the board may belong to the same political party. At least three members of the board shall be from judicial districts other than the First, Second, Fourth, and Tenth Judicial Districts. To the extent practicable, the membership of the board must include persons with disabilities, reflect the ethnic diversity of the state, take into consideration race and gender, and include persons from throughout the state. The members shall be well acquainted with representing parents in district court and appellate proceedings related to child protection matters as well as the law that affects a parent attorney's work, including chapter 260C, the Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure, the Rules of Civil Appellate Procedure, the Indian Child Welfare Act, and the Minnesota Indian Family Preservation Act. The terms, compensation, and removal of members shall be as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>. The members shall elect a chair from among the membership and the chair shall serve a term of two years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Head appellate counsel for parents; assistant and contracted attorneys; other employees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning January 1, 2024, and for every four years after that date, the board shall appoint a head appellate counsel in charge of executing the responsibilities of the office who shall provide for sufficient appellate counsel for parents and other personnel necessary to discharge the functions of the office. The head appellate counsel shall serve a four-year term and may be removed only for cause upon the order of the board. The head appellate counsel shall be a full-time qualified attorney, licensed to practice law in this state, and serve in the unclassified service of the state. Vacancies of the office shall be filled by the appointing authority for the unexpired term. The head appellate counsel shall devote full time to the performance of duties and shall not engage in the general practice of law. The compensation of the head appellate counsel shall be set by the board and shall be commensurate with county attorneys in the state.</p>
          <p>(b) Consistent with the decisions of the board, the head appellate counsel shall employ assistants or hire independent contractors to serve as appellate counsel for parents. Each assistant appellate counsel and independent contractor serves at the pleasure of the head appellate counsel. The compensation of assistant appellate counsel and independent contractors shall be set by the board and shall be commensurate with county attorneys in the state.</p>
          <p>(c) A person serving as appellate counsel shall be a qualified attorney licensed to practice law in this state. A person serving as appellate counsel practicing in Tribal court shall be a licensed attorney qualified to practice law in Tribal courts in the state. Assistant appellate counsel and contracted appellate counsel may engage in the general practice of law where not employed or contracted to provide services on a full-time basis.</p>
          <p>(d) The head appellate counsel shall, consistent with the responsibilities under subdivision 2, employ or hire the following:</p>
          <p>(1) one managing appellate attorney;</p>
          <p>(2) two staff attorneys;</p>
          <p>(3) one director of training;</p>
          <p>(4) one program administrator to support Title IV-E reimbursement in collaboration with the Department of Children, Youth, and Families; and</p>
          <p>(5) one office administrator.</p>
          <p>(e) Each employee identified in paragraph (d) serves at the pleasure of the head appellate counsel. The compensation of each employee shall be set by the board and shall be commensurate with county attorneys in the state.</p>
          <p>(f) Any person serving as managing appellate attorney, staff attorney, and director of training shall be a qualified attorney licensed to practice law in the state.</p>
          <p>(g) A person serving as the program administrator and office administrator must be chosen solely on the basis of training, experience, and qualifications.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duties and responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board shall work cooperatively with the head appellate counsel to govern the office and provide fiscal oversight.</p>
          <p>(b) The board shall approve and recommend to the legislature a budget for the board, the office, and any programs operated by that office.</p>
          <p>(c) The board shall establish procedures for distribution of funding under this section to the office and any programs operated by that office.</p>
          <p>(d) The head appellate counsel with the approval of the board shall establish appellate program standards, administrative policies, procedures, and rules consistent with statute, rules of court, and laws that affect appellate counsel's work. The standards must include but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) standards needed to maintain and operate an appellate counsel for parents program, including requirements regarding the qualifications, training, and size of the legal and supporting staff for an appellate counsel program;</p>
          <p>(2) standards for appellate counsel caseloads;</p>
          <p>(3) standards and procedures for the eligibility of appointment, assessment, and collection of the costs for legal representation provided by appellate counsel;</p>
          <p>(4) standards for contracts between contracted appellate counsel and the state appellate counsel program for the legal representation of indigent persons;</p>
          <p>(5) standards prescribing minimum qualifications of counsel appointed under the board's authority or by the courts; and</p>
          <p>(6) standards ensuring the independent, competent, and efficient representation of clients whose cases present conflicts of interest.</p>
          <p>(e) The head appellate counsel, with approval of the board, shall establish training program standards and processes and procedures necessary to carry out the office's responsibilities for statewide training of parent attorneys, including but not limited to establishing uniform practice standards and training requirements for all parent attorneys practicing in the state.</p>
          <p>(f) The head appellate counsel and the program administrator with approval of the board shall establish processes and procedures for collaborating with the Department of Children, Youth, and Families to secure and utilize Title IV-E funds and communicating with counties and Tribes and any other processes and procedures necessary to carry out the office's responsibilities.</p>
          <p>(g) The board may:</p>
          <p>(1) propose statutory changes to the legislature and rule changes to the supreme court that are in the best interests of the operation of the appellate counsel for parents program; and</p>
          <p>(2) require the reporting of statistical data, budget information, and other cost factors by the appellate counsel for parents program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>In no event shall the board or its members interfere with the discretion, judgment, or zealous advocacy of counsel in their handling of individual cases as a part of the judicial branch of government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Budget; county and Tribe use.</span></h2>
          <p>The establishment of the office and its employees and support staff and the board shall be funded by the state of Minnesota. Minnesota counties and Tribes may utilize this office to provide appellate representation to indigent parents in their jurisdiction who are seeking an appeal and for assistance in securing Title IV-E funding through collaboration with the Department of Children, Youth, and Families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.419.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Collection of costs; appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>If any of the costs provided by appellate counsel are assessed and collected or otherwise reimbursed from any source, the State Board of Appellate Counsel and Training shall deposit payments in a separate account established in the special revenue fund. The amount credited to this account is appropriated to the State Board of Appellate Counsel and Training. The balance of this account does not cancel but is available until expended.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.419.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.3.1.0">2023 c 52 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.421">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.421 CONTEMPT.</h1>
        <p>Any person knowingly interfering with an order of the juvenile court is in contempt of court. However, a child who is under the continuing jurisdiction of the court for reasons other than having committed a delinquent act or a juvenile petty offense may not be adjudicated as a delinquent solely on the basis of having knowingly interfered with or disobeyed an order of the court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.421.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.42.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 42</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.425">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.425 CRIMINAL JURISDICTION FOR CONTRIBUTING TO NEED FOR PROTECTION OR SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.425.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Crimes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any person who by act, word, or omission encourages, causes, or contributes to the need for protection or services is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
          <p>(b) This section does not apply to licensed social services agencies and outreach workers who, while acting within the scope of their professional duties, provide services to runaway children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.425.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Complaint; venue.</span></h2>
          <p>A complaint under this section may be filed by the county attorney having jurisdiction where the child is found, resides, or where the alleged act of contributing occurred. The complaint may be filed in either the juvenile or criminal divisions of the district court. A prior or pending petition alleging that the child is delinquent, a juvenile petty offender, or in need of protection or services is not a prerequisite to a complaint or a conviction under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.425.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Affirmative defense.</span></h2>
          <p>If the child's conduct is the basis for the child's need for protection services, it is an affirmative defense to a prosecution under subdivision 1 if the defendant proves, by a preponderance of the evidence, that the defendant took reasonable steps to control the child's conduct.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.425.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.43.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 43</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.431"><b>260C.431</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/361/#laws.6.59.0">2008 c 361 art 6 s 59</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.435"><b>260C.435</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/361/#laws.6.59.0">2008 c 361 art 6 s 59</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COSTS AND PAYMENTS</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.441"><b>260C.441</b> MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.24.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 24</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.4411">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.4411 PRE-NORTHSTAR CARE FOR CHILDREN FOSTER CARE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.4411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For a child placed in family foster care on or before December 31, 2014, the county of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02">256G.02</a> or tribal agency authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>, shall pay the local share under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.611#stat.142A.611.3">142A.611, subdivision 3</a>, for foster care maintenance including any difficulty of care as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9560.0521">9560.0521</a>, subparts 7 and 10. Family foster care includes:</p>
          <p>(1) emergency relative placement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.06">142B.06</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) licensed foster family settings, foster residence settings, or treatment foster care settings, licensed under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3000">2960.3000</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3340">2960.3340</a>, and served by a public or private child care agency authorized by Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9545.0755">9545.0755</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9545.0845">9545.0845</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) family foster care homes approved by a tribal agency; and</p>
          <p>(4) unlicensed supervised settings for foster youth ages 18 to 21.</p>
          <p>(b) The county of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02">256G.02</a> or tribal social services agency authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>, shall pay the entire cost of any initial clothing allowance, administrative payments to child care agencies specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.907">317A.907</a>, or any other support services it authorizes, except as otherwise provided by law.</p>
          <p>(c) The rates for the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program remain those in effect on January 1, 2013, continuing the preexisting rate structure for foster children who remain with the same caregivers and do not transition into Northstar Care for Children under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604#stat.142A.604.6">142A.604, subdivision 6</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Difficulty of care payments must be maintained consistent with Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9560.0652">9560.0652</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9560.0653">9560.0653</a>, using the established reassessment tool in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9560.0654">9560.0654</a>. The preexisting rate structure for the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program must be maintained, provided that when the number of foster children in the program is less than ten percent of the population in 2012, the commissioner may apply the same assessment tool to both the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program and Northstar Care for Children under the authority granted in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.2">142A.607, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) The county of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02">256G.02</a> or tribal agency authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>, shall document the determined pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care rate in the case record, including a description of each condition on which the difficulty of care assessment is based. The difficulty of care rate is reassessed:</p>
          <p>(1) every 12 months;</p>
          <p>(2) at the request of the foster parent; or</p>
          <p>(3) if the child's level of need changes in the current foster home.</p>
          <p>(f) The pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program must maintain the following existing program features:</p>
          <p>(1) monthly payments must be made to the family foster home provider;</p>
          <p>(2) notice and appeal procedures must be consistent with Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9560.0665">9560.0665</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) medical assistance eligibility for foster children must continue to be determined according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.055">256B.055</a>.</p>
          <p>(g) The county of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02">256G.02</a> or tribal agency authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>, may continue existing program features, including:</p>
          <p>(1) establishing a local fund of county money through which the agency may reimburse foster parents for the cost of repairing damage done to the home and contents by the foster child and the additional care insurance premium cost of a child who possesses a permit or license to drive a car; and</p>
          <p>(2) paying a fee for specific services provided by the foster parent, based on the parent's skills, experience, or training. This fee must not be considered foster care maintenance.</p>
          <p>(h) The following events end the child's enrollment in the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program:</p>
          <p>(1) reunification with parent or other relative;</p>
          <p>(2) adoption or transfer of permanent legal and physical custody;</p>
          <p>(3) removal from the current foster home to a different foster home;</p>
          <p>(4) another event that ends the current placement episode; or</p>
          <p>(5) attaining the age of 21.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.4411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consideration of other programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When a child in foster care is eligible to receive a grant of Retirement Survivors Disability Insurance (RSDI) or Supplemental Security Income for the aged, blind, and disabled, or a foster care maintenance payment under title IV-E of the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, sections 670 to 676, the child's needs must be met through these programs. Every effort must be made to establish a child's eligibility for a title IV-E grant to reimburse the county or tribe from the federal funds available for this purpose.</p>
          <p>(b) When a child in foster care qualifies for home and community-based waivered services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.49">256B.49</a> for community alternative care (CAC), community access for disability inclusion (CADI), or traumatic brain injury (TBI) waivers, this service does not substitute for the child foster care program. When a foster child is receiving waivered services benefits, the county of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02">256G.02</a> or tribal agency authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>, assesses and provides foster care maintenance including difficulty of care using the established tool in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9560.0654">9560.0654</a>. If it is determined that additional services are needed to meet the child's needs in the home that are not or cannot be met by the foster care program, the needs must be referred to the waivered service program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.4411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.20.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.6.31.0">2015 c 78 art 6 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 2 s 74; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>,41</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.4412">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.4412 PAYMENT FOR RESIDENTIAL PLACEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) When a child is placed in a foster care group residential setting under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0020">2960.0020</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0710">2960.0710</a>, a foster residence licensed under chapter 245A that meets the standards of Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3200">2960.3200</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3230">2960.3230</a>, or a children's residential facility licensed or approved by a tribe, foster care maintenance payments must be made on behalf of the child to cover the cost of providing food, clothing, shelter, daily supervision, school supplies, child's personal incidentals and supports, reasonable travel for visitation, or other transportation needs associated with the items listed. Daily supervision in the group residential setting includes routine day-to-day direction and arrangements to ensure the well-being and safety of the child. It may also include reasonable costs of administration and operation of the facility.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner of human services shall specify the title IV-E administrative procedures under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.418">142A.418</a> for each of the following residential program settings:</p>
        <p>(1) residential programs licensed under chapter 245A or licensed by a tribe, including:</p>
        <p>(i) qualified residential treatment programs as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.26d">260C.007, subdivision 26d</a>;</p>
        <p>(ii) program settings specializing in providing prenatal, postpartum, or parenting supports for youth; and</p>
        <p>(iii) program settings providing high-quality residential care and supportive services to children and youth who are, or are at risk of becoming, sex trafficking victims;</p>
        <p>(2) licensed residential family-based substance use disorder treatment programs as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.22a">260C.007, subdivision 22a</a>; and</p>
        <p>(3) supervised settings in which a foster child age 18 or older may live independently, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.451">260C.451</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) A lead contract under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.07#stat.142A.07.6">142A.07, subdivision 6</a>, is not required to establish the foster care maintenance payment in paragraph (a) for foster residence settings licensed under chapter 245A that meet the standards of Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3200">2960.3200</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3230">2960.3230</a>. The foster care maintenance payment for these settings must be consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.3">142A.609, subdivision 3</a>, and subject to the annual revision as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256N.26#stat.256N.26.9">256N.26, subdivision 9</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.4412.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.21.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.82.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.35.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.4413">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.4413 INITIAL CLOTHING ALLOWANCE.</h1>
        <p>(a) An initial clothing allowance must be available to a child eligible for:</p>
        <p>(1) the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.4411">260C.4411</a>, subdivision 1; and</p>
        <p>(2) the Northstar Care for Children benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604">142A.604</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) An initial clothing allowance must also be available for a foster child in a group residential setting based on the child's individual needs during the first 60 days of the child's initial placement. The agency must consider the parent's ability to provide for a child's clothing needs and the residential facility contracts.</p>
        <p>(c) The county of financial responsibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256G.02">256G.02</a> or tribal agency authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03">142A.03</a>, subdivision 9, shall approve an initial clothing allowance consistent with the child's needs. The amount of the initial clothing allowance must not exceed the monthly basic rate for the child's age group under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>, subdivision 3.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.4413.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.22.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.446">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.446 DISTRIBUTION OF FUNDS RECOVERED FOR ASSISTANCE FURNISHED.</h1>
        <p>When any amount shall be recovered from any source for assistance furnished under the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.001">260C.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.421">260C.421</a>, there shall be paid into the treasury of the state or county in the proportion in which they have respectively contributed toward the total assistance paid.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.446.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.47.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/86/#laws.1.46.0">2009 c 86 art 1 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.23.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FOSTER CARE BENEFITS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.451">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.451 FOSTER CARE BENEFITS PAST AGE 18.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notification.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> Six months prior to the child's 18th birthday, the responsible social services agency shall provide written notice on a form prescribed by the commissioner of children, youth, and families to any child in foster care under this chapter who cannot reasonably be expected to return home or have another legally permanent family by the age of 18, the child's parents or legal guardian, if any, the child's guardian ad litem, and the child's foster parents of the availability of foster care up to age 21, when the child is eligible under subdivisions 3 and 3a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Independent living plan.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Upon the request of any child in foster care immediately prior to the child's 18th birthday and who is in foster care at the time of the request, the responsible social services agency shall, in conjunction with the child and other appropriate parties, update the independent living plan required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (c), clause (12), related to the child's employment, vocational, educational, social, or maturational needs. The agency shall provide continued services and foster care for the child including those services that are necessary to implement the independent living plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility to continue in foster care.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A child in foster care immediately prior to the child's 18th birthday may continue in foster care past age 18 unless:</p>
          <p>(1) the child can safely return home;</p>
          <p>(2) the child is in placement pursuant to the agency's duties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092 </a>and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.0004">9525.0004</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.0016">9525.0016</a>, to meet the child's needs due to a developmental disability or related condition, and the child will be served as an adult under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092</a> and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.0004">9525.0004</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9525.0016">9525.0016</a>; or</p>
          <p>(3) the child can be adopted or have permanent legal and physical custody transferred to a relative prior to the child's 18th birthday.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Eligibility criteria.</span></h2>
          <p>The child must meet at least one of the following conditions to be considered eligible to continue in or return to foster care and remain there to age 21. The child must be:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) completing secondary education or a program leading to an equivalent credential;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) enrolled in an institution that provides postsecondary or vocational education;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) participating in a program or activity designed to promote or remove barriers to employment;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) employed for at least 80 hours per month; or</p>
          <p class="in">(5) incapable of doing any of the activities described in clauses (1) to (4) due to a medical condition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Foster care benefits.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For children between the ages of 18 and 21, "foster care benefits" means payment for those foster care settings defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 18. Additionally, foster care benefits means payment for a supervised setting, approved by the responsible social services agency, in which a child may live independently.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Foster care setting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The particular foster care setting, including supervised settings, shall be selected by the agency and the child based on the best interest of the child consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>. Supervision in approved settings must be determined by an individual determination of the child's needs by the responsible social services agency and consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.4a">260C.212, subdivision 4a</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Reentering foster care and accessing services after 18 years of age and up to 21 years of age.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Upon request of an individual who had been under the guardianship of the commissioner and who has left foster care without being adopted, the responsible social services agency which had been the commissioner's agent for purposes of the guardianship shall develop with the individual a plan to increase the individual's ability to live safely and independently using the plan requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), clause (12), and to assist the individual to meet one or more of the eligibility criteria in subdivision 4 if the individual wants to reenter foster care. The responsible social services agency shall provide foster care as required to implement the plan. The responsible social services agency shall enter into a voluntary placement agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.229">260C.229</a> with the individual if the plan includes foster care.</p>
          <p>(b) Individuals who had not been under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families prior to 18 years of age may ask to reenter foster care after age 18 and the responsible social services agency that had responsibility for planning for the individual before discharge from foster care shall provide foster care or other services to the individual for the purpose of increasing the individual's ability to live safely and independently and to meet the eligibility criteria in subdivision 3a, if the individual:</p>
          <p>(1) was in foster care for the six consecutive months prior to the person's 18th birthday, or left foster care within six months prior to the person's 18th birthday, and was not discharged home, adopted, or received into a relative's home under a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.4">260C.515, subdivision 4</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) was discharged from foster care while on runaway status after age 15.</p>
          <p>(c) In conjunction with a qualifying and eligible individual under paragraph (b) and other appropriate persons, the responsible social services agency shall develop a specific plan related to that individual's vocational, educational, social, or maturational needs and provide foster care as required to implement the plan. The responsible social services agency shall enter into a voluntary placement agreement with the individual if the plan includes foster care.</p>
          <p>(d) A child who left foster care while under guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families retains eligibility for foster care for placement at any time prior to 21 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Jurisdiction.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> Individuals in foster care pursuant to this section are adults for all purposes except the continued provision of foster care. Any order establishing guardianship under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325">260C.325</a>, any legal custody order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.1">260C.201, subdivision 1</a>, and any order for legal custody associated with an order for permanent custody under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.5">260C.515, subdivision 5</a>, terminates on the child's 18th birthday. The responsible social services agency has legal responsibility for the individual's placement and care when the matter continues under court jurisdiction pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.193">260C.193</a> or when the individual and the responsible agency execute a voluntary placement agreement pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.229">260C.229</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notice of termination of foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>When a child in foster care between the ages of 18 and 21 ceases to meet one of the eligibility criteria of subdivision 3a, the responsible social services agency shall give the child written notice that foster care will terminate 30 days from the date the notice is sent. The child or the child's guardian ad litem may file a motion asking the court to review the agency's determination within 15 days of receiving the notice. The child shall not be discharged from foster care until the motion is heard. The agency shall work with the child to transition out of foster care as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a>, paragraph (d), clause (2). The written notice of termination of benefits shall be on a form prescribed by the commissioner and shall also give notice of the right to have the agency's determination reviewed by the court in the proceeding where the court conducts the reviews required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.317">260C.317</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a>, subdivision 5 or 6. A copy of the termination notice shall be sent to the child and the child's attorney, if any, the foster care provider, the child's guardian ad litem, and the court. The agency is not responsible for paying foster care benefits for any period of time after the child actually leaves foster care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.451.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Administrative or court review of placements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court shall conduct reviews at least annually to ensure the responsible social services agency is making reasonable efforts to finalize the permanency plan for the child.</p>
          <p>(b) The court shall find that the responsible social services agency is making reasonable efforts to finalize the permanency plan for the child when the responsible social services agency:</p>
          <p>(1) provides appropriate support to the child and foster care provider to ensure continuing stability and success in placement;</p>
          <p>(2) works with the child to plan for transition to adulthood and assists the child in demonstrating progress in achieving related goals;</p>
          <p>(3) works with the child to plan for independent living skills and assists the child in demonstrating progress in achieving independent living goals; and</p>
          <p>(4) prepares the child for independence according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a>, paragraph (d), and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.452#stat.260C.452.4">260C.452, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The responsible social services agency must ensure that an administrative review that meets the requirements of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a> is completed at least six months after each of the court's annual reviews.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.451.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/139/#laws.3.48.0">1999 c 139 art 3 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/269/#laws.3.9.0">2010 c 269 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/301/#laws.3.9.0">2010 c 301 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.24.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.63.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 63</a>,64; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.15.11.0">2016 c 189 art 15 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.91.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 91</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.7.32.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 7 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.452">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.452 SUCCESSFUL TRANSITION TO ADULTHOOD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.452.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, "youth" means a person who is at least 14 years of age and under 23 years of age.</p>
          <p>(b) This section pertains to a youth who:</p>
          <p>(1) is in foster care and is 14 years of age or older, including a youth who is under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families;</p>
          <p>(2) has a permanency disposition of permanent custody to the agency;</p>
          <p>(3) will leave foster care when the youth is 18 years of age or older and under 21 years of age;</p>
          <p>(4) has left foster care due to adoption when the youth was 16 years of age or older;</p>
          <p>(5) has left foster care due to a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative, or Tribal equivalent, when the youth was 16 years of age or older; or</p>
          <p>(6) was reunified with the youth's primary caretaker when the youth was 14 years of age or older and under 18 years of age.</p>
          <p>(c) The purpose of this section is to provide support to each youth who is transitioning to adulthood by providing services to the youth in the areas of:</p>
          <p>(1) education;</p>
          <p>(2) employment;</p>
          <p>(3) daily living skills such as financial literacy training and driving instruction, preventive health activities including promoting abstinence from substance use and smoking, and nutrition education and pregnancy prevention;</p>
          <p>(4) forming meaningful, permanent connections with caring adults;</p>
          <p>(5) engaging in age-appropriate and developmentally appropriate activities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.14">260C.212, subdivision 14</a>, and positive youth development;</p>
          <p>(6) financial, housing, counseling, and other services to assist a youth over 18 years of age in achieving self-sufficiency and accepting personal responsibility for the transition from adolescence to adulthood; and</p>
          <p>(7) making vouchers available for education and training.</p>
          <p>(d) The responsible social services agency may provide support and case management services to a youth as defined in paragraph (a) until the youth reaches 23 years of age. According to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.451">260C.451</a>, a youth's placement in a foster care setting will end when the youth reaches 21 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.452.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Case management services.</span></h2>
          <p>Case management services include the responsibility for planning, coordinating, authorizing, monitoring, and evaluating services for a youth and shall be provided to a youth by the responsible social services agency or the contracted agency. Case management services include the out-of-home placement plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, when the youth is in out-of-home placement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.452.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Independent living plan.</span></h2>
          <p>When the youth is 14 years of age or older and is receiving support from the responsible social services agency under this section, the responsible social services agency, in consultation with the youth, shall complete the youth's independent living plan according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), clause (12), regardless of the youth's current placement status.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.452.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2020 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.36.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.452.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administrative or court review of placements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When the youth is 14 years of age or older, the court, in consultation with the youth, shall review the youth's independent living plan according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a>, paragraph (d).</p>
          <p>(b) The responsible social services agency shall file a copy of the notification of foster care benefits for a youth who is 18 years of age or older according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.451#stat.260C.451.1">260C.451, subdivision 1</a>, with the court. If the responsible social services agency does not file the notice by the time the youth is 17-1/2 years of age, the court shall require the responsible social services agency to file the notice.</p>
          <p>(c) When a youth is 18 years of age or older, the court shall ensure that the responsible social services agency assists the youth in obtaining the following documents before the youth leaves foster care: a Social Security card; an official or certified copy of the youth's birth certificate; a state identification card or driver's license, tribal enrollment identification card, green card, or school visa; health insurance information; the youth's school, medical, and dental records; a contact list of the youth's medical, dental, and mental health providers; and contact information for the youth's siblings, if the siblings are in foster care.</p>
          <p>(d) For a youth who will be discharged from foster care at 18 years of age or older because the youth is not eligible for extended foster care benefits or chooses to leave foster care, the responsible social services agency must develop a personalized transition plan as directed by the youth during the 180-day period immediately prior to the expected date of discharge. The transition plan must be as detailed as the youth elects and include specific options, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) affordable housing with necessary supports that does not include a homeless shelter;</p>
          <p>(2) health insurance, including eligibility for medical assistance as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.055#stat.256B.055.17">256B.055, subdivision 17</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) education, including application to the Education and Training Voucher Program;</p>
          <p>(4) local opportunities for mentors and continuing support services;</p>
          <p>(5) workforce supports and employment services;</p>
