          <p>(6) a copy of the youth's consumer credit report as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13C.001">13C.001 </a>and assistance in interpreting and resolving any inaccuracies in the report, at no cost to the youth;</p>
          <p>(7) information on executing a health care directive under chapter 145C and on the importance of designating another individual to make health care decisions on behalf of the youth if the youth becomes unable to participate in decisions;</p>
          <p>(8) appropriate contact information through 21 years of age if the youth needs information or help dealing with a crisis situation; and</p>
          <p>(9) official documentation that the youth was previously in foster care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.452.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Notice of termination of social services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before a youth who is 18 years of age or older leaves foster care, the responsible social services agency shall give the youth written notice that foster care shall terminate 30 days from the date that the notice is sent by the agency according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.451#stat.260C.451.8">260C.451, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Before case management services will end for a youth who is at least 18 years of age and under 23 years of age, the responsible social services agency shall give the youth: (1) written notice that case management services for the youth shall terminate; and (2) written notice that the youth has the right to appeal the termination of case management services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.20#stat.142A.20.2">142A.20, subdivision 2</a>, by responding in writing within ten days of the date that the agency mailed the notice. The termination notice must include information about services for which the youth is eligible and how to access the services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.452.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.15.13.0">2016 c 189 art 15 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.32.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.36.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.456"><b>260C.456</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.6.14.0">2012 c 216 art 6 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.501"><b>260C.501</b> MS 2010 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.177">260C.177</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PERMANENCY PROCEEDINGS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.503">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.503 PERMANENCY PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.503.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Required permanency proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except for children in foster care pursuant to chapter 260D, where the child is in foster care or in the care of a noncustodial or nonresident parent, the court shall commence proceedings to determine the permanent status of a child by holding the admit-deny hearing required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.507">260C.507</a> not later than 12 months after the child is placed in foster care or in the care of a noncustodial or nonresident parent. Permanency proceedings for children in foster care pursuant to chapter 260D shall be according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260D.07">260D.07</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Permanency proceedings for a foster child who is colocated with a parent in a licensed residential family-based substance use disorder treatment program shall be conducted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.190">260C.190</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.503.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Termination of parental rights.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The responsible social services agency must ask the county attorney to immediately file a termination of parental rights petition when:</p>
          <p>(1) the child has been subjected to egregious harm as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 14;</p>
          <p>(2) the child is determined to be the sibling of a child who was subjected to egregious harm;</p>
          <p>(3) the child is an abandoned infant as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301#stat.260C.301.2">260C.301, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2);</p>
          <p>(4) the child's parent has lost parental rights to another child through an order involuntarily terminating the parent's rights;</p>
          <p>(5) the parent has committed sexual abuse as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.03">260E.03</a>, against the child or another child of the parent;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) the parent has committed an offense that requires registration as a predatory offender under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/243.166#stat.243.166.1b">243.166, subdivision 1b</a>, paragraph (a) or (b); or</p>
          <p>(7) another child of the parent is the subject of an order involuntarily transferring permanent legal and physical custody of the child to a relative under this chapter or a similar law of another jurisdiction.</p>
          <p class="bl">The county attorney shall file a termination of parental rights petition unless the conditions of paragraph (d) are met.</p>
          <p>(b) When the termination of parental rights petition is filed under this subdivision, the responsible social services agency shall identify, recruit, and approve an adoptive family for the child. If a termination of parental rights petition has been filed by another party, the responsible social services agency shall be joined as a party to the petition.</p>
          <p>(c) If criminal charges have been filed against a parent arising out of the conduct alleged to constitute egregious harm, the county attorney shall determine which matter should proceed to trial first, consistent with the best interests of the child and subject to the defendant's right to a speedy trial.</p>
          <p>(d) The requirement of paragraph (a) does not apply if the responsible social services agency and the county attorney determine and file with the court:</p>
          <p>(1) a petition for transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.505">260C.505</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.4">260C.515, subdivision 4</a>, including a determination that adoption is not in the child's best interests and that transfer of permanent legal and physical custody is in the child's best interests; or</p>
          <p>(2) a petition under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.141">260C.141</a> alleging the child, and where appropriate, the child's siblings, to be in need of protection or services accompanied by a case plan prepared by the responsible social services agency documenting a compelling reason why filing a termination of parental rights petition would not be in the best interests of the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.503.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Calculating time to required permanency proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the date of the child's placement in foster care is the earlier of the first court-ordered placement or 60 days after the date on which the child has been voluntarily placed in foster care by the child's parent or guardian. For purposes of this section, time spent by a child in the home of the noncustodial parent pursuant to court order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.178">260C.178</a> or under the protective supervision of the responsible social services agency in the home of the noncustodial parent pursuant to an order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.1">260C.201, subdivision 1</a>, counts towards the requirement of a permanency hearing under this section. Time spent on a trial home visit counts towards the requirement of a permanency hearing under this section and the permanency progress review required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.204">260C.204</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this section, 12 months is calculated as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) during the pendency of a petition alleging that a child is in need of protection or services, all time periods when a child is placed in foster care or in the home of a noncustodial parent are cumulated; and</p>
          <p>(2) if a child has been placed in foster care within the previous five years under one or more previous petitions, the lengths of all prior time periods when the child was placed in foster care within the previous five years are cumulated. If a child under this clause has been in foster care for 12 months or more, the court, if it is in the best interests of the child and for compelling reasons, may extend the total time the child may continue out of the home under the current petition up to an additional six months before making a permanency determination.</p>
          <p>(c) If the child is on a trial home visit 12 months after the child was placed in foster care or in the care of a noncustodial parent, the responsible social services agency may file a report with the court regarding the child's and parent's progress on the trial home visit and the agency's reasonable efforts to finalize the child's safe and permanent return to the care of the parent in lieu of filing the petition required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.505">260C.505</a>. The court shall make findings regarding the reasonable efforts of the agency to finalize the child's return home as the permanency disposition order in the best interests of the child. The court may continue the trial home visit to a total time not to exceed six months as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.201#stat.260C.201.1">260C.201, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (3). If the court finds the agency has not made reasonable efforts to finalize the child's return home as the permanency disposition order in the child's best interests, the court may order other or additional efforts to support the child remaining in the care of the parent. If a trial home visit ordered or continued at permanency proceedings under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a> terminates, the court shall commence or recommence permanency proceedings under this chapter no later than 30 days after the child is returned to foster care or to the care of a noncustodial parent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.503.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Qualified residential treatment program; permanency hearing requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>When a child is placed in a qualified residential treatment program as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.26d">260C.007, subdivision 26d</a>, the responsible social services agency must submit evidence to the court as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.712">260C.712</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.503.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.25.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/125/#laws.1.58.0">2013 c 125 art 1 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.1.33.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.83.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 83</a>; art 8 s 126; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.37.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.505">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.505 PETITION.</h1>
        <p>(a) A permanency or termination of parental rights petition must be filed at or prior to the time the child has been in foster care or in the care of a noncustodial or nonresident parent for 11 months or in the expedited manner required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a>, subdivision 2, paragraph (a). The court administrator shall serve the petition as required in the Minnesota Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.152">260C.152</a> for the admit-deny hearing on the petition required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.507">260C.507</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) A petition under this section is not required if the responsible social services agency intends to recommend that the child return to the care of the parent from whom the child was removed at or prior to the time the court is required to hold the admit-deny hearing required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.507">260C.507</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.505.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.26.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 26</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.507">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.507 ADMIT-DENY HEARING.</h1>
        <p>(a) An admit-deny hearing on the permanency or termination of parental rights petition shall be held not later than 12 months from the child's placement in foster care or an order for the child to be in the care of a noncustodial or nonresident parent.</p>
        <p>(b) An admit-deny hearing on the termination of parental rights or transfer of permanent legal and physical custody petition required to be immediately filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503#stat.260C.503.2">260C.503, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a), shall be within ten days of the filing of the petition.</p>
        <p>(c) At the admit-deny hearing, the court shall determine whether there is a prima facie basis for finding that the agency made reasonable efforts, or in the case of an Indian child active efforts, for reunification as required or that reasonable efforts for reunification are not required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.012">260.012</a> and proceed according to the Minnesota Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.507.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.27.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 27</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.509">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.509 TRIAL.</h1>
        <p>The permanency proceedings shall be conducted in a timely fashion including that any trial required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163">260C.163</a> shall be commenced within 60 days of the admit-deny hearing required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.507">260C.507</a>. At the conclusion of the permanency proceedings, the court shall:</p>
        <p>(1) order the child returned to the care of the parent or guardian from whom the child was removed; or</p>
        <p>(2) order a permanency disposition under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a> or termination of parental rights under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301">260C.301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.328">260C.328</a> if a permanency disposition order or termination of parental rights is in the child's best interests.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.509.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.28.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 28</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.511">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.511 BEST INTERESTS OF THE CHILD.</h1>
        <p>(a) The "best interests of the child" means all relevant factors to be considered and evaluated. In the case of an Indian child, best interests of the child includes best interests of an Indian child as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.755#stat.260.755.2a">260.755, subdivision 2a</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) In making a permanency disposition order or termination of parental rights, the court must be governed by the best interests of the child, including a review of the relationship between the child and relatives and the child and other important persons with whom the child has resided or had significant contact.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.511.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.29.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.1.34.0">2015 c 78 art 1 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.513">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.513 PERMANENCY DISPOSITIONS WHEN CHILD CANNOT RETURN HOME.</h1>
        <p>(a) For a child who cannot return home, a permanency placement with a relative is preferred. A permanency placement with a relative includes termination of parental rights and adoption by a relative, guardianship to the commissioner of children, youth, and families through a consent to adopt with a relative, or a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative. The court must consider the best interests of the child and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 2, paragraph (a), when making a permanency determination.</p>
        <p>(b) When the court has determined that permanent placement of the child away from the parent is necessary, the court shall consider permanent alternative homes that are available both inside and outside the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.513.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.30.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.8.23.0">2022 c 98 art 8 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.515">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.515 PERMANENCY DISPOSITION ORDERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.515.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Court order required.</span></h2>
          <p>If the child is not returned to the home at or before the conclusion of permanency proceedings under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503">260C.503</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>, the court must order one of the permanency dispositions in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.515.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Termination of parental rights.</span></h2>
          <p>The court may order:</p>
          <p>(1) termination of parental rights when the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301">260C.301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.328">260C.328</a> are met; or</p>
          <p>(2) the responsible social services agency to file a petition for termination of parental rights in which case all the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.301">260C.301</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.328">260C.328 </a>remain applicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.515.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Guardianship; commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The court may issue an order that the child is under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families under the following procedures and conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) there is an identified prospective adoptive parent agreed to by the responsible social services agency that has legal custody of the child pursuant to court order under this chapter and that prospective adoptive parent has agreed to adopt the child;</p>
          <p>(2) the court accepts the parent's voluntary consent to adopt in writing on a form prescribed by the commissioner, executed before two competent witnesses and confirmed by the consenting parent before the court or executed before the court. The consent shall contain notice that consent given under this chapter:</p>
          <p>(i) is irrevocable upon acceptance by the court unless fraud is established and an order is issued permitting revocation as stated in clause (9) unless the matter is governed by the Indian Child Welfare Act, United States Code, title 25, section 1913(c); and</p>
          <p>(ii) will result in an order that the child is under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families;</p>
          <p>(3) a consent executed and acknowledged outside of this state, either in accordance with the law of this state or in accordance with the law of the place where executed, is valid;</p>
          <p>(4) the court must review the matter at least every 90 days under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.317">260C.317</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) a consent to adopt under this subdivision vests guardianship of the child with the commissioner of children, youth, and families and makes the child a ward of the commissioner of children, youth, and families under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325">260C.325</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) the court must forward to the commissioner a copy of the consent to adopt, together with a certified copy of the order transferring guardianship to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(7) if an adoption is not finalized by the identified prospective adoptive parent within six months of the execution of the consent to adopt under this clause, the responsible social services agency shall pursue adoptive placement in another home unless the court finds in a hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.317">260C.317</a> that the failure to finalize is not due to either an action or a failure to act by the prospective adoptive parent;</p>
          <p>(8) notwithstanding clause (7), the responsible social services agency must pursue adoptive placement in another home as soon as the agency determines that finalization of the adoption with the identified prospective adoptive parent is not possible, that the identified prospective adoptive parent is not willing to adopt the child, or that the identified prospective adoptive parent is not cooperative in completing the steps necessary to finalize the adoption. The court may order a termination of parental rights under subdivision 2; and</p>
          <p>(9) unless otherwise required by the Indian Child Welfare Act, United States Code, title 25, section 1913(c), a consent to adopt executed under this section shall be irrevocable upon acceptance by the court except upon order permitting revocation issued by the same court after written findings that consent was obtained by fraud.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.515.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Transfer of permanent legal and physical custody.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court may order a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to:</p>
          <p>(1) a parent. The court must find that the parent understands a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody includes permanent, ongoing responsibility for the protection, education, care, and control of the child and decision making on behalf of the child until adulthood; or</p>
          <p>(2) a fit and willing relative according to the requirements in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) An order for transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative must only be made after the court has reviewed the suitability of the prospective legal and physical custodian, including a summary of information obtained from required background studies under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.209">260C.209</a>, if the court finds the permanency disposition to be in the child's best interests.</p>
          <p> In transferring permanent legal and physical custody to a relative, the juvenile court shall follow the standards applicable under this chapter and chapter 260, and the procedures in the Minnesota Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure. The court must issue written findings that include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the prospective legal and physical custodian understands that:</p>
          <p>(i) a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody includes permanent, ongoing responsibility for the protection, education, care, and control of the child and decision making on behalf of the child until adulthood; and</p>
          <p>(ii) a permanent legal and physical custodian shall not return a child to the permanent care of a parent from whom the court removed custody without the court's approval and without notice to the responsible social services agency;</p>
          <p>(2) transfer of permanent legal and physical custody and receipt of Northstar kinship assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>, when requested and the child is eligible, are in the child's best interests;</p>
          <p>(3) when the agency files the petition under paragraph (c) or supports the petition filed under paragraph (d), adoption is not in the child's best interests based on the determinations in the kinship placement agreement required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.605#stat.142A.605.2">142A.605, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) the agency made efforts to discuss adoption with the child's parent or parents, or the agency did not make efforts to discuss adoption and the reasons why efforts were not made; and</p>
          <p>(5) there are reasons to separate siblings during placement, if applicable.</p>
          <p>(c) The responsible social services agency may file a petition naming a fit and willing relative as a proposed permanent legal and physical custodian. A petition for transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative shall include facts upon which the court can determine suitability of the proposed custodian, including a summary of results from required background studies completed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a>. The petition must be accompanied by a kinship placement agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.605#stat.142A.605.2">142A.605, subdivision 2</a>, between the agency and proposed permanent legal and physical custodian.</p>
          <p>(d) Another party to the permanency proceeding regarding the child may file a petition to transfer permanent legal and physical custody to a relative. The petition must include facts upon which the court can make the determinations required under paragraph (b), including suitability of the proposed custodian and, if completed, a summary of results from required background studies completed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.209">260C.209</a>. If background studies have not been completed at the time of filing the petition, they must be completed and a summary of results provided to the court prior to the court granting the petition or finalizing the order according to paragraph (e). The petition must be filed no later than the date for the required admit-deny hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.507">260C.507</a>; or if the agency's petition is filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.503#stat.260C.503.2">260C.503, subdivision 2</a>, the petition must be filed not later than 30 days prior to the trial required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.509">260C.509</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) The court may:</p>
          <p>(1) defer finalization of an order transferring permanent legal and physical custody to a relative when deferring finalization is necessary to determine eligibility for Northstar kinship assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) finalize a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative regardless of eligibility for Northstar kinship assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>, provided that the court has reviewed the suitability of the proposed custodian, including the summary of background study results, consistent with paragraph (b); and</p>
          <p>(3) following a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative, maintain jurisdiction over the responsible social services agency, the parents or guardian of the child, the child, and the permanent legal and physical custodian for purposes of ensuring appropriate services are delivered to the child and permanent legal custodian for the purpose of ensuring conditions ordered by the court related to the care and custody of the child are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.515.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Permanent custody to agency.</span></h2>
          <p>The court may order permanent custody to the responsible social services agency for continued placement of the child in foster care but only if it approves the responsible social services agency's compelling reasons that no other permanency disposition order is in the child's best interests and:</p>
          <p>(1) the child has reached age 16 and has been asked about the child's desired permanency outcome;</p>
          <p>(2) the responsible social services agency has made reasonable efforts to locate and place the child with an adoptive family or a fit and willing relative who would either agree to adopt the child or to a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody of the child, but these efforts have not proven successful; and</p>
          <p>(3) the parent will continue to have visitation or contact with the child and will remain involved in planning for the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.515.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Temporary legal custody to agency.</span></h2>
          <p>The court may order temporary legal custody to the responsible social services agency for continued placement of the child in foster care for a specified period of time according to the following conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) the sole basis for an adjudication that the child is in need of protection or services is the child's behavior;</p>
          <p>(2) the court finds that foster care for a specified period of time is in the best interests of the child;</p>
          <p>(3) the court approves the responsible social services agency's compelling reasons that neither an award of permanent legal and physical custody to a relative nor termination of parental rights is in the child's best interests; and</p>
          <p>(4) the order specifies that the child continue in foster care no longer than one year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.515.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.31.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.25.30.0">2014 c 312 art 25 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.65.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.38.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>; art 18 s 39</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.517">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.517 FINDINGS AND CONTENT OF ORDER FOR PERMANENCY DISPOSITION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except for an order terminating parental rights, an order permanently placing a child out of the home of the parent or guardian must include the following detailed findings:</p>
        <p>(1) how the child's best interests are served by the order;</p>
        <p>(2) the nature and extent of the responsible social services agency's reasonable efforts or, in the case of an Indian child, active efforts to reunify the child with the parent or guardian where reasonable efforts are required;</p>
        <p>(3) the parent's or parents' efforts and ability to use services to correct the conditions which led to the out-of-home placement; and</p>
        <p>(4) that the conditions which led to the out-of-home placement have not been corrected so that the child can safely return home.</p>
        <p>(b) The court shall issue an order required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a> and this section within 15 days of the close of the proceedings. The court may extend issuing the order an additional 15 days when necessary in the interests of justice and the best interests of the child.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.517.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.32.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 32</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.519">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.519 FURTHER COURT HEARINGS.</h1>
        <p>Once a permanency disposition order has been made, further court hearings are necessary if:</p>
        <p>(1) the child is ordered on a trial home visit or under the protective supervision of the responsible social services agency;</p>
        <p>(2) the child continues in foster care;</p>
        <p>(3) the court orders further hearings in a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody matter including if a party seeks to modify an order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>, subdivision 2;</p>
        <p>(4) an adoption has not yet been finalized; or</p>
        <p>(5) the child returns to foster care after the court has entered an order for a permanency disposition under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.519.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.33.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 33</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.521">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.521 COURT REVIEWS AFTER PERMANENCY DISPOSITION ORDER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.521.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Child in permanent custody of responsible social services agency.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Court reviews of an order for permanent custody to the responsible social services agency for placement of the child in foster care must be conducted at least yearly at an in-court appearance hearing.</p>
          <p>(b) The purpose of the review hearing is to ensure:</p>
          <p>(1) the responsible social services agency made intensive, ongoing, and, as of the date of the hearing, unsuccessful efforts to return the child home or secure a placement for the child with a fit and willing relative, custodian, or adoptive parent, and an order for permanent custody to the responsible social services agency for placement of the child in foster care continues to be in the best interests of the child;</p>
          <p>(2) that the responsible social services agency is assisting the child to build connections to the child's family and community;</p>
          <p>(3) that the responsible social services agency is appropriately planning with the child for development of independent living skills for the child and, as appropriate, for the orderly and successful transition to adulthood that may occur if the child continues in foster care without another permanency disposition order;</p>
          <p>(4) the child's foster family home or child care institution is following the reasonable and prudent parenting standards; and</p>
          <p>(5) the child has regular, ongoing opportunities to engage in age or developmentally appropriate activities by consulting with the child in an age-appropriate manner about the opportunities.</p>
          <p>(c) The court must review the child's out-of-home placement plan and the reasonable efforts of the responsible social services agency to finalize an alternative permanent plan for the child including the responsible social services agency's efforts to:</p>
          <p>(1) ensure that permanent custody to the responsible social services agency with placement of the child in foster care continues to be the most appropriate legal arrangement for meeting the child's need for permanency and stability by reviewing the compelling reasons it continues not to be in the best interest of the child to:</p>
          <p>(i) return home;</p>
          <p>(ii) be placed for adoption; or</p>
          <p>(iii) be placed with a fit and willing relative through an order for permanent legal and physical custody under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.4">260C.515, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) identify a specific foster home for the child, if one has not already been identified;</p>
          <p>(3) support continued placement of the child in the identified home, if one has been identified;</p>
          <p>(4) ensure appropriate services are provided to address the physical health, mental health, and educational needs of the child during the period of foster care and also ensure appropriate services or assistance to maintain relationships with appropriate family members and the child's community; and</p>
          <p>(5) plan for the child's independence upon the child's leaving foster care living as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The court may find that the responsible social services agency has made reasonable efforts to finalize the permanent plan for the child when:</p>
          <p>(1) the responsible social services agency has made reasonable efforts to identify a more legally permanent home for the child than is provided by an order for permanent custody to the agency for placement in foster care;</p>
          <p>(2) the child has been asked about the child's desired permanency outcome; and</p>
          <p>(3) the responsible social services agency's engagement of the child in planning for a successful transition to adulthood is reasonable and appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.521.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Modifying order for permanent legal and physical custody to a relative.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An order for a relative to have permanent legal and physical custody of a child may be modified using standards under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/518.18">518.18</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/518.185">518.185</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) When a child is receiving Northstar kinship assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>, if a relative named as permanent legal and physical custodian in an order made under this chapter becomes incapacitated or dies, a successor custodian named in the Northstar Care for Children kinship assistance benefit agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a> may file a request to modify the order for permanent legal and physical custody to name the successor custodian as the permanent legal and physical custodian of the child. The court may modify the order to name the successor custodian as the permanent legal and physical custodian upon reviewing the background study required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a> if the court finds the modification is in the child's best interests.</p>
          <p>(c) The social services agency is a party to the proceeding and must receive notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.521.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Modifying order for permanent custody to agency for placement in foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A parent may seek modification of an order for permanent custody of the child to the responsible social services agency for placement in foster care upon motion and a showing by the parent of a substantial change in the parent's circumstances such that the parent could provide appropriate care for the child and that removal of the child from the permanent custody of the agency and the return to the parent's care would be in the best interests of the child.</p>
          <p>(b) The responsible social services agency may ask the court to vacate an order for permanent custody to the agency upon a petition and hearing pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163">260C.163 </a>establishing the basis for the court to order another permanency disposition under this chapter, including termination of parental rights based on abandonment if the parent has not visited the child, maintained contact with the child, or participated in planning for the child as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.5">260C.515, subdivision 5</a>. The responsible social services agency must establish that the proposed permanency disposition order is in the child's best interests. Upon a hearing where the court determines the petition is proved, the court may vacate the order for permanent custody and enter a different order for a permanent disposition that is in the child's best interests. The court shall not require further reasonable efforts to reunify the child with the parent or guardian as a basis for vacating the order for permanent custody to the agency and ordering a different permanency disposition in the child's best interests. The county attorney must file the petition and give notice as required under the Minnesota Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure in order to modify an order for permanent custody under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.521.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Change in placement.</span></h2>
          <p>If a child is removed from a permanent placement disposition authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.4">260C.515, subdivision 4, 5, or 6</a>, within one year after the placement was made:</p>
          <p>(1) the child must be returned to the residential facility where the child was placed immediately preceding the permanent placement; or</p>
          <p>(2) the court shall hold a hearing within ten days after the child is removed from the permanent placement to determine where the child is to be placed. A guardian ad litem must be appointed for the child for this hearing.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.521.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.8.23.0">1999 c 245 art 8 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.4.34.0">2012 c 216 art 4 s 34; art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.66.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 66</a>,67; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.15.14.0">2016 c 189 art 15 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ADOPTION OF CHILDREN UNDER<br>GUARDIANSHIP OF COMMISSIONER</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.601">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.601 ADOPTION OF CHILDREN UNDER GUARDIANSHIP OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.601.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Review and finalization requirements; adoption procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.601">260C.601</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.635">260C.635</a> establish:</p>
          <p>(1) the requirements for court review of children under the guardianship of the commissioner; and</p>
          <p>(2) procedures for timely finalizing adoptions in the best interests of children under the guardianship of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) Adoption proceedings for children not under the guardianship of the commissioner are governed by chapter 259.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.601.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duty of responsible agency.</span></h2>
          <p>The responsible social services agency has the duty to act as the commissioner's agent in making reasonable efforts to finalize the adoption of all children under the guardianship of the commissioner pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325">260C.325</a>. In implementing these duties, the agency shall ensure that:</p>
          <p>(1) the best interests of the child are met in the planning and granting of adoptions;</p>
          <p>(2) a child under the guardianship of the commissioner is appropriately involved in planning for adoption;</p>
          <p>(3) the diversity of Minnesota's population and diverse needs including culture, religion, and language of persons affected by adoption are recognized and respected; and</p>
          <p>(4) the court has the timely information it needs to make a decision that is in the best interests of the child in reviewing the agency's planning for adoption and when ordering the adoption of the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.601.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Background study.</span></h2>
          <p>Consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a> and United States Code, title 42, section 671, a completed background study is required before the adoptive placement of the child in a related or an unrelated home.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.601.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.26.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 26</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.603">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.603 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.601">260C.601</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.635">260C.635</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Adopting parent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Adopting parent" means an adult who has signed an adoption placement agreement regarding the child and has the same meaning as preadoptive parent under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259A.01#stat.259A.01.23">259A.01, subdivision 23</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Adoption placement agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Adoption placement agreement" means the written agreement between the responsible social services agency, the commissioner, and the adopting parent which reflects the intent of all the signatories to the agreement that the adopting parent establish a parent and child relationship by adoption with the child who is under the guardianship of the commissioner. The adoptive placement agreement must be in the commissioner's designated format.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Adoptive parent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Adoptive parent" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259A.01#stat.259A.01.3">259A.01, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Adoptive placement.</span></h2>
          <p>"Adoptive placement" means a placement made by the responsible social services agency upon a fully executed adoption placement agreement including the signatures of the adopting parent, the responsible social services agency, and the commissioner of children, youth, and families according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.613#stat.260C.613.1">260C.613, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of children, youth, and families or any employee of the Department of Children, Youth, and Families to whom the commissioner has delegated authority regarding children under the commissioner's guardianship.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Guardianship.</span></h2>
          <p>"Guardianship" has the meaning given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259A.01">259A.01</a>, subdivision 17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325">260C.325</a>; or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.3">260C.515, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.603.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Prospective adoptive parent.</span></h2>
          <p>"Prospective adoptive parent" means an individual who may become an adopting parent regardless of whether the individual has an adoption study approving the individual for adoption, but who has not signed an adoption placement agreement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.603.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.27.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.605">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.605 REASONABLE EFFORTS TO FINALIZE AN ADOPTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.605.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Reasonable efforts to finalize the adoption of a child under the guardianship of the commissioner shall be made by the responsible social services agency responsible for permanency planning for the child.</p>
          <p>(b) Reasonable efforts to make a placement in a home according to the placement considerations under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, with a relative or foster parent who will commit to being the permanent resource for the child in the event the child cannot be reunified with a parent are required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.012">260.012</a> and may be made concurrently with reasonable, or if the child is an Indian child, active efforts to reunify the child with the parent.</p>
          <p>(c) Reasonable efforts under paragraph (b) must begin as soon as possible when the child is in foster care under this chapter, but not later than the hearing required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.204">260C.204</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Reasonable efforts to finalize the adoption of the child include:</p>
          <p>(1) considering the child's preference for an adoptive family;</p>
          <p>(2) using age-appropriate engagement strategies to plan for adoption with the child;</p>
          <p>(3) identifying an appropriate prospective adoptive parent for the child by updating the child's identified needs using the factors in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) making an adoptive placement that meets the child's needs by:</p>
          <p>(i) completing or updating the relative search required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a> and giving notice of the need for an adoptive home for the child to:</p>
          <p>(A) relatives who have kept the agency or the court apprised of their whereabouts; or</p>
          <p>(B) relatives of the child who are located in an updated search;</p>
          <p>(ii) an updated search is required whenever:</p>
          <p>(A) there is no identified prospective adoptive placement for the child notwithstanding a finding by the court that the agency made diligent efforts under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>, in a hearing required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.202">260C.202</a>;</p>
          <p>(B) the child is removed from the home of an adopting parent; or</p>
          <p>(C) the court determines that a relative search by the agency is in the best interests of the child;</p>
          <p>(iii) engaging the child's relatives or current or former foster parents to commit to being the prospective adoptive parent of the child, and considering the child's relatives for adoptive placement of the child in the order specified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a); or</p>
          <p>(iv) when there is no identified prospective adoptive parent:</p>
          <p>(A) registering the child on the state adoption exchange as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.75">259.75 </a>unless the agency documents to the court an exception to placing the child on the state adoption exchange reported to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(B) reviewing all families with approved adoption home studies associated with the responsible social services agency;</p>
          <p>(C) presenting the child to adoption agencies and adoption personnel who may assist with finding an adoptive home for the child;</p>
          <p>(D) using newspapers and other media to promote the particular child;</p>
          <p>(E) using a private agency under grant contract with the commissioner to provide adoption services for intensive child-specific recruitment efforts; and</p>
          <p>(F) making any other efforts or using any other resources reasonably calculated to identify a prospective adoption parent for the child;</p>
          <p>(5) updating and completing the social and medical history required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.15">260C.212, subdivision 15</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.609">260C.609</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) making, and keeping updated, appropriate referrals required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.851">260.851</a>, the Interstate Compact on the Placement of Children;</p>
          <p>(7) giving notice regarding the responsibilities of an adoptive parent to any prospective adoptive parent as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.35">259.35</a>;</p>
          <p>(8) offering the adopting parent the opportunity to apply for or decline adoption assistance under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>;</p>
          <p>(9) certifying the child for adoption assistance, assessing the amount of adoption assistance, and ascertaining the status of the commissioner's decision on the level of payment if the adopting parent has applied for adoption assistance;</p>
          <p>(10) placing the child with siblings. If the child is not placed with siblings, the agency must document reasonable efforts to place the siblings together, as well as the reason for separation. The agency may not cease reasonable efforts to place siblings together for final adoption until the court finds further reasonable efforts would be futile or that placement together for purposes of adoption is not in the best interests of one of the siblings; and</p>
          <p>(11) working with the adopting parent to file a petition to adopt the child and with the court administrator to obtain a timely hearing to finalize the adoption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.605.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">No waiver.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The responsible social services agency shall make reasonable efforts to recruit, assess, and match an adoptive home for any child under the guardianship of the commissioner and reasonable efforts shall continue until an adoptive placement is made and adoption finalized or until the child is no longer under the guardianship of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) A child of any age who is under the guardianship of the commissioner and is legally available for adoption may not refuse or waive the responsible social services agency's reasonable efforts to recruit, identify, and place the child in an adoptive home required under this section. The agency has an ongoing responsibility to work with the child to explore the child's opportunities for adoption, and what adoption means for the child, and may not accept a child's refusal to consider adoption as an option.</p>
          <p>(c) The court may not relieve or otherwise order the responsible social services agency to cease fulfilling the responsible social services agency's duty regarding reasonable efforts to recruit, identify, and place the child in an adoptive home.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.605.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.28.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.39.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.8.24.0">2022 c 98 art 8 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.607">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.607 REVIEW OF PROGRESS TOWARD ADOPTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Review hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court shall conduct a review of the responsible social services agency's reasonable efforts to finalize adoption for any child under the guardianship of the commissioner and of the progress of the case toward adoption at least every 90 days after the court issues an order that the commissioner is the guardian of the child.</p>
          <p>(b) The review of progress toward adoption shall continue notwithstanding that an appeal is made of the order for guardianship or termination of parental rights.</p>
          <p>(c) The agency's reasonable efforts to finalize the adoption must continue during the pendency of the appeal under paragraph (b) or subdivision 6, paragraph (h), and all progress toward adoption shall continue except that the court may not finalize an adoption while the appeal is pending.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice of review hearings shall be given by the court to:</p>
          <p>(1) the responsible social services agency;</p>
          <p>(2) the child, if the child is age ten and older;</p>
          <p>(3) the child's guardian ad litem;</p>
          <p>(4) counsel appointed for the child pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.163#stat.260C.163.3">260C.163, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) relatives of the child who have kept the court informed of their whereabouts as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a> and who have responded to the agency's notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>, unless the relative has been previously ruled out by the court as a suitable permanency resource for the child;</p>
          <p>(6) the current foster or adopting parent of the child;</p>
          <p>(7) any foster or adopting parents of siblings of the child; and</p>
          <p>(8) the Indian child's tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Right to participate.</span></h2>
          <p>Any individual or entity listed in subdivision 2 may participate in the continuing reviews conducted under this section. No other individual or entity is required to be given notice or to participate in the reviews unless the court specifically orders that notice be given or participation in the reviews be required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Content of review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court shall review:</p>
          <p>(1) the agency's reasonable efforts under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.605">260C.605</a> to finalize an adoption for the child as appropriate to the stage of the case; and</p>
          <p>(2) the child's current out-of-home placement plan required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 1, to ensure the child is receiving all services and supports required to meet the child's needs as they relate to the child's:</p>
          <p>(i) placement;</p>
          <p>(ii) visitation and contact with siblings;</p>
          <p>(iii) visitation and contact with relatives;</p>
          <p>(iv) medical, mental, and dental health; and</p>
          <p>(v) education.</p>
          <p>(b) When the child is age 14 and older, and as long as the child continues in foster care, the court shall also review the agency's planning for the child's independent living after leaving foster care including how the agency is meeting the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), clause (12). The court shall use the review requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a> in any review conducted under this paragraph.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Required placement by responsible social services agency.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No petition for adoption shall be filed for a child under the guardianship of the commissioner unless the child sought to be adopted has been placed for adoption with the adopting parent by the responsible social services agency as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.613#stat.260C.613.1">260C.613, subdivision 1</a>. The court may order the agency to make an adoptive placement using standards and procedures under subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(b) Any relative or the child's foster parent who believes the responsible agency has not reasonably considered the relative's or foster parent's request to be considered for adoptive placement as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, and who wants to be considered for adoptive placement of the child shall bring a request for consideration to the attention of the court during a review required under this section. The child's guardian ad litem and the child may also bring a request for a relative or the child's foster parent to be considered for adoptive placement. After hearing from the agency, the court may order the agency to take appropriate action regarding the relative's or foster parent's request for consideration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Motion and hearing to order adoptive placement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) At any time after the district court orders the child under the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families, but not later than 30 days after receiving notice required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.613">260C.613</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (c), that the agency has made an adoptive placement, a relative or the child's foster parent may file a motion for an order for adoptive placement of a child who is under the guardianship of the commissioner if the relative or the child's foster parent:</p>
          <p>(1) has an adoption home study under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.41">259.41 </a>or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.611">260C.611 </a>approving the relative or foster parent for adoption. If the relative or foster parent does not have an adoption home study, an affidavit attesting to efforts to complete an adoption home study may be filed with the motion instead. The affidavit must be signed by the relative or foster parent and the responsible social services agency or licensed child-placing agency completing the adoption home study. The relative or foster parent must also have been a resident of Minnesota for at least six months before filing the motion; the court may waive the residency requirement for the moving party if there is a reasonable basis to do so; or</p>
          <p>(2) is not a resident of Minnesota, but has an approved adoption home study by an agency licensed or approved to complete an adoption home study in the state of the individual's residence and the study is filed with the motion for adoptive placement. If the relative or foster parent does not have an adoption home study in the relative or foster parent's state of residence, an affidavit attesting to efforts to complete an adoption home study may be filed with the motion instead. The affidavit must be signed by the relative or foster parent and the agency completing the adoption home study.</p>
          <p>(b) The motion must be filed with the court conducting reviews of the child's progress toward adoption under this section. The motion and supporting documents must make a prima facie showing that the agency has been unreasonable in failing to make the requested adoptive placement. The motion must be served according to the requirements for motions under the Minnesota Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure and must be made on all individuals and entities listed in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(c) If the motion and supporting documents do not make a prima facie showing for the court to determine whether the agency has been unreasonable in failing to make the requested adoptive placement, the court shall dismiss the motion. If the court determines a prima facie basis is made, the court shall set the matter for evidentiary hearing.</p>
          <p>(d) At the evidentiary hearing, the responsible social services agency shall proceed first with evidence about the reason for not making the adoptive placement proposed by the moving party. When the agency presents evidence regarding the child's current relationship with the identified adoptive placement resource, the court must consider the agency's efforts to support the child's relationship with the moving party consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>. The moving party then has the burden of proving by a preponderance of the evidence that the agency has been unreasonable in failing to make the adoptive placement.</p>
          <p>(e) The court shall review and enter findings regarding whether the agency, in making an adoptive placement decision for the child:</p>
          <p>(1) considered relatives for adoptive placement in the order specified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a); and</p>
          <p>(2) assessed how the identified adoptive placement resource and the moving party are each able to meet the child's current and future needs, based on an individualized determination of the child's needs, as required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.613#stat.260C.613.1">260C.613, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(f) At the conclusion of the evidentiary hearing, if the court finds that the agency has been unreasonable in failing to make the adoptive placement and that the moving party is the most suitable adoptive home to meet the child's needs using the factors in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b), the court may:</p>
          <p>(1) order the responsible social services agency to make an adoptive placement in the home of the moving party if the moving party has an approved adoption home study; or</p>
          <p>(2) order the responsible social services agency to place the child in the home of the moving party upon approval of an adoption home study. The agency must promote and support the child's ongoing visitation and contact with the moving party until the child is placed in the moving party's home. The agency must provide an update to the court after 90 days, including progress and any barriers encountered. If the moving party does not have an approved adoption home study within 180 days, the moving party and the agency must inform the court of any barriers to obtaining the approved adoption home study during a review hearing under this section. If the court finds that the moving party is unable to obtain an approved adoption home study, the court must dismiss the order for adoptive placement under this subdivision and order the agency to continue making reasonable efforts to finalize the adoption of the child as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.605">260C.605</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) If, in order to ensure that a timely adoption may occur, the court orders the responsible social services agency to make an adoptive placement under this subdivision, the agency shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) make reasonable efforts to obtain a fully executed adoption placement agreement, including assisting the moving party with the adoption home study process;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) work with the moving party regarding eligibility for adoption assistance as required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) if the moving party is not a resident of Minnesota, timely refer the matter for approval of the adoptive placement through the Interstate Compact on the Placement of Children.</p>
          <p>(h) Denial or granting of a motion for an order for adoptive placement after an evidentiary hearing is an order that may be appealed by the responsible social services agency, the moving party, the child, when age ten or over, the child's guardian ad litem, and any individual who had a fully executed adoption placement agreement regarding the child at the time the motion was filed if the court's order has the effect of terminating the adoption placement agreement. An appeal must be conducted according to the requirements of the Rules of Juvenile Protection Procedure. Pursuant to subdivision 1, paragraph (c), the court shall not finalize an adoption while an appeal is pending.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Changing adoptive plan when parent has consented to adoption.</span></h2>
          <p>When the child's parent has consented to adoption under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.3">260C.515, subdivision 3</a>, only the person identified by the parent and agreed to by the agency as the prospective adoptive parent qualifies for adoptive placement of the child until the responsible social services agency has reported to the court and the court has found in a hearing under this section that it is not possible to finalize an adoption by the identified prospective adoptive parent within 12 months of the execution of the consent to adopt under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a>, subdivision 3, unless the responsible social services agency certifies that the failure to finalize is not due to either an action or a failure to act by the prospective adoptive parent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.607.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Timing modified.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court may review the responsible social services agency's reasonable efforts to finalize an adoption more frequently than every 90 days whenever a more frequent review would assist in finalizing the adoption.</p>
          <p>(b) In appropriate cases, the court may review the responsible social services agency's reasonable efforts to finalize an adoption less frequently than every 90 days. The court shall not find it appropriate to review progress toward adoption less frequently than every 90 days except when:</p>
          <p>(1) the court has approved the agency's reasonable efforts to recruit, identify, and place the child in an adoptive home on a continuing basis for at least 24 months after the court has issued the order for guardianship;</p>
          <p>(2) the child is at least 16 years old; and</p>
          <p>(3) the child's guardian ad litem agrees that review less frequently than every 90 days is in the child's best interests.</p>
          <p>(c) In no event shall the court's review be less frequent than every six months.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.607.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.29.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.68.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/60/#laws.0.3.0">2017 c 60 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.40.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.8.25.0">2022 c 98 art 8 s 25</a>-27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>; art 18 s 40,41</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.609">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.609 SOCIAL AND MEDICAL HISTORY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The responsible social services agency shall thoroughly discuss the child's history with the prospective adoptive parent of the child and shall give a redacted copy of the child's social and medical history as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 15, including redacted attachments, to the prospective adoptive parent. If the prospective adoptive parent does not pursue adoption of the child, the prospective adoptive parent must return the child's social and medical history and redacted attachments to the agency.
