The responsible social services agency may give a redacted copy of the child's social and medical history to the child according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) The report shall not include information that identifies birth relatives. Redacted copies of all of the child's relevant evaluations, assessments, and records must be attached to the social and medical history.</p>
        <p>(c) The agency must submit the child's social and medical history to the Department of Human Services at the time that the agency submits the child's adoption placement agreement. Pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.623#stat.260C.623.4">260C.623, subdivision 4</a>, the child's social and medical history must be submitted to the court at the time the adoption petition is filed with the court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.609.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.30.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.41.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 41</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.611">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.611 ADOPTION STUDY REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>(a) An adoption study under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.41">259.41</a> approving placement of the child in the home of the prospective adoptive parent must be completed before placing any child under the guardianship of the commissioner in a home for adoption. If a prospective adoptive parent has a current child foster care license under chapter 142B and is seeking to adopt a foster child who is placed in the prospective adoptive parent's home and is under the guardianship of the commissioner according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325#stat.260C.325.1">260C.325, subdivision 1</a>, the child foster care home study meets the requirements of this section for an approved adoption home study if:</p>
        <p>(1) the written home study on which the foster care license was based is completed in the commissioner's designated format, consistent with the requirements in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.41#stat.259.41.2">259.41, subdivision 2</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.215#stat.260C.215.4">260C.215, subdivision 4</a>, clause (5); and Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3060">2960.3060</a>, subpart 4;</p>
        <p>(2) the background studies on each prospective adoptive parent and all required household members were completed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) the commissioner has not issued a sanction on the license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.18">142B.18</a> or an order of a conditional license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.16">142B.16</a> within the last three years, or the commissioner has determined it to be in the child's best interests to allow the child foster care home study to meet requirements of an approved adoption home study upon review of the legally responsible agency's adoptive placement decision; and</p>
        <p>(4) the legally responsible agency determines that the individual needs of the child are being met by the prospective adoptive parent through an assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.2">142A.607, subdivision 2</a>, or a documented placement decision consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) If a prospective adoptive parent has previously held a foster care license or adoptive home study, any update necessary to the foster care license, or updated or new adoptive home study, if not completed by the licensing authority responsible for the previous license or home study, shall include collateral information from the previous licensing or approving agency, if available.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.611.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.31.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.25.31.0">2014 c 312 art 25 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 2 s 74; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34,</a>41; art 18 s 42</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.613">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.613 SOCIAL SERVICES AGENCY AS COMMISSIONER'S AGENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Adoptive placement decisions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The responsible social services agency has exclusive authority to make an adoptive placement decision for a child under the guardianship of the commissioner. The child is legally placed for adoption when the adopting parent, the agency, and the commissioner have fully executed an adoption placement agreement on the form prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The responsible social services agency shall use an individualized determination of the child's current and future needs, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b), to determine the most suitable adopting parent for the child in the child's best interests. The responsible social services agency must consider adoptive placement of the child with relatives in the order specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) The responsible social services agency shall notify the court and parties entitled to notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607#stat.260C.607.2">260C.607, subdivision 2</a>, when there is a fully executed adoption placement agreement for the child.</p>
          <p>(d) Pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.615#stat.260C.615.1">260C.615, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), clause (4), the responsible social services agency shall immediately notify the commissioner if the agency learns of any new or previously undisclosed criminal or maltreatment information involving an adoptive placement of a child under guardianship of the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(e) In the event a party to an adoption placement agreement terminates the agreement, the responsible social services agency shall notify the court, the parties entitled to notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607#stat.260C.607.2">260C.607, subdivision 2</a>, and the commissioner that the agreement and the adoptive placement have terminated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Disclosure of data permitted to identify adoptive parent.</span></h2>
          <p>The responsible social services agency may disclose private data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02">13.02</a>, to prospective adoptive parents for the purpose of identifying an adoptive parent willing and able to meet the child's needs as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Siblings placed together.</span></h2>
          <p>The responsible social services agency shall place siblings together for adoption according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.012">260.012</a>, paragraph (e), clause (4), unless:</p>
          <p>(1) the court makes findings required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.617">260C.617</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) the court orders that the adoption or progress toward adoption of the child under the court's jurisdiction may proceed notwithstanding that the adoption will result in siblings being separated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Other considerations.</span></h2>
          <p>Placement of a child cannot be delayed or denied based on the race, color, or national origin of the prospective parent or the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Required record keeping.</span></h2>
          <p>The responsible social services agency shall document, in the records required to be kept under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.79">259.79</a>, the reasons for the adoptive placement decision regarding the child, including the individualized determination of the child's needs based on the factors in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>, paragraph (b); the agency's consideration of relatives in the order specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212, subdivision </a>2, paragraph (a); and the assessment of how the selected adoptive placement meets the identified needs of the child. The responsible social services agency shall retain in the records required to be kept under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.79">259.79</a>, copies of all out-of-home placement plans made since the child was ordered under guardianship of the commissioner and all court orders from reviews conducted pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Death notification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agency shall inform the adoptive parents that the adoptive parents of an adopted child under age 19 or an adopted person age 19 or older may maintain a current address on file with the agency and indicate a desire to be notified if the agency receives information of the death of a birth parent. The agency shall notify birth parents of the child's death and the cause of death, if known, provided that the birth parents desire notice and maintain current addresses on file with the agency. The agency shall inform birth parents entitled to notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.27">259.27</a> that they may designate individuals to notify the agency if a birth parent dies and that the agency receiving information of the birth parent's death will share the information with adoptive parents, if the adopted person is under age 19, or an adopted person age 19 or older who has indicated a desire to be notified of the death of a birth parent and who maintains a current address on file with the agency.</p>
          <p>(b) Notice to a birth parent that a child has died or to the adoptive parents or an adopted person age 19 or older that a birth parent has died shall be provided by an employee of the agency through personal and confidential contact, but not by mail.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Terminal illness notification.</span></h2>
          <p>If a birth parent or the child is terminally ill, the responsible social services agency shall inform the adoptive parents and birth parents of a child who is adopted that the birth parents, the adoptive parents of an adopted person under age 19, or an adopted person age 19 or older may request to be notified of the terminal illness. The agency shall notify the other parties if a request is received under this subdivision and upon a party's request the agency shall share information regarding a terminal illness with the adoptive or birth parents or an adopted person age 19 or older.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.613.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Postadoption search services.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The responsible social services agency shall respond to requests from adopted persons age 19 years and over, adoptive parents of a minor child, and birth parents for social and medical history and genetic health conditions of the adopted person's birth family and genetic sibling information according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.83">259.83</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.613.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.32.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.8.28.0">2022 c 98 art 8 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.18.43.0">2024 c 115 art 18 s 43</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.615">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.615 DUTIES OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.615.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For any child who is under the guardianship of the commissioner, the commissioner has the exclusive rights to consent to:</p>
          <p>(1) the medical care plan for the treatment of a child who is at imminent risk of death or who has a chronic disease that, in a physician's judgment, will result in the child's death in the near future including a physician's order not to resuscitate or intubate the child; and</p>
          <p>(2) the child donating a part of the child's body to another person while the child is living; the decision to donate a body part under this clause shall take into consideration the child's wishes and the child's culture.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the exclusive rights under paragraph (a), the commissioner has a duty to:</p>
          <p>(1) process any complete and accurate request for home study and placement through the Interstate Compact on the Placement of Children under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.851">260.851</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) process any complete and accurate application for adoption assistance forwarded by the responsible social services agency according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) review and process an adoption placement agreement forwarded to the commissioner by the responsible social services agency and return it to the agency in a timely fashion;</p>
          <p>(4) review new or previously undisclosed information received from the agency or other individuals or entities that may impact the health, safety, or well-being of a child who is the subject of a fully executed adoption placement agreement; and</p>
          <p>(5) maintain records as required in chapter 259.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.615.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties not reserved.</span></h2>
          <p>All duties, obligations, and consents not specifically reserved to the commissioner in this section are delegated to the responsible social services agency, subject to supervision by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/393.07">393.07</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.615.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.33.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.42.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>; art 18 s 44</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.617">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.617 SIBLING PLACEMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The responsible social services agency shall make every effort to place siblings together for adoption.</p>
        <p>(b) The court shall review any proposal by the responsible social services agency to separate siblings for purposes of adoption.</p>
        <p>(c) If there is venue in more than one county for matters regarding siblings who are under the guardianship of the commissioner, the judges conducting reviews regarding the siblings shall communicate with each other about the siblings' needs and, where appropriate, shall conduct review hearings in a manner that ensures coordinated planning by agencies involved in decision making for the siblings.</p>
        <p>(d) After notice to the individuals and entities listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.627">260C.627</a>, the foster or prospective adoptive parent of the child, and any foster, adopting, or adoptive parents of the child's siblings, or relatives with permanent legal and physical custody of the child's sibling, and upon hearing, the court may determine that a child under the court's jurisdiction may be separated from the child's sibling for adoption when:</p>
        <p>(1) the responsible social services agency has made reasonable efforts to place the siblings together, and after finding reasonable efforts have been made, the court finds further efforts would significantly delay the adoption of one or more of the siblings and are therefore not in the best interests of one or more of the siblings; or</p>
        <p>(2) the court determines it is not in the best interests of one or more of the siblings to be placed together after reasonable efforts by the responsible social services agency to place the siblings together.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.617.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.34.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 34</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.619">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.619 COMMUNICATION AND CONTACT AGREEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) An adopting parent and a relative or foster parent of the child may enter into an agreement regarding communication with or contact between the adopted child, adopting parent, and the relative or foster parent. An agreement may be entered between:</p>
        <p>(1) an adopting parent and a birth parent;</p>
        <p>(2) an adopting parent and any relative or foster parent with whom the child resided before being adopted; and</p>
        <p>(3) an adopting parent and the parent or legal custodian of a sibling of the child, if the sibling is a minor, or any adult sibling of the child.</p>
        <p>(b) An agreement regarding communication with or contact between the child, adoptive parents, and a relative or foster parent, is enforceable when the terms of the agreement are contained in a written court order. The order must be issued before or at the time of the granting of the decree of adoption. The order granting the communication, contact, or visitation shall be filed in the adoption file.</p>
        <p>(c) The court shall mail a certified copy of the order to the parties to the agreement or their representatives at the addresses provided by the parties to the agreement. Service shall be completed in a manner that maintains the confidentiality of confidential information.</p>
        <p>(d) The court shall not enter a proposed order unless the terms of the order have been approved in writing by the prospective adoptive parents, the birth relative, the foster parent, or the birth parent or legal custodian of the child's sibling who desires to be a party to the agreement, and the responsible social services agency.</p>
        <p>(e) An agreement under this section need not disclose the identity of the parties to be legally enforceable and when the identity of the parties to the agreement is not disclosed, data about the identities in the adoption file shall remain confidential.</p>
        <p>(f) The court shall not enter a proposed order unless the court finds that the communication or contact between the minor adoptee, the adoptive parents, and the relative, foster parents, or siblings as agreed upon and contained in the proposed order, would be in the child's best interests.</p>
        <p>(g) Failure to comply with the terms of an order regarding communication or contact that has been entered by the court under this section is not grounds for:</p>
        <p>(1) setting aside an adoption decree; or</p>
        <p>(2) revocation of a written consent to an adoption after that consent has become irrevocable.</p>
        <p>(h) An order regarding communication or contact entered under this section may be enforced by filing a motion in the existing adoption file with the court that entered the contact agreement. Any party to the communication or contact order or the child who is the subject of the order has standing to file the motion to enforce the order. The prevailing party may be awarded reasonable attorney fees and costs.</p>
        <p>(i) The court shall not modify an order under this section unless it finds that the modification is necessary to serve the best interests of the child, and:</p>
        <p>(1) the modification is agreed to by the parties to the agreement; or</p>
        <p>(2) exceptional circumstances have arisen since the order was entered that justified modification of the order.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.619.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.35.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 35</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.621">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.621 JURISDICTION AND VENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.621.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Jurisdiction.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The juvenile court has original jurisdiction for all adoption proceedings involving the adoption of a child under the guardianship of the commissioner, including when the commissioner approves the placement of the child through the Interstate Compact on the Placement of Children under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.851">260.851</a> for adoption outside the state of Minnesota and an adoption petition is filed in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) The receiving state also has jurisdiction to conduct an adoption proceeding for a child under the guardianship of the commissioner when the adopting home was approved by the receiving state through the interstate compact.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.621.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Venue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Venue for the adoption of a child committed to the guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families shall be the court conducting reviews in the matter according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon request of the responsible social services agency, the court conducting reviews under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a> may order that filing an adoption petition involving a child under the guardianship of the commissioner be permitted in the county where the adopting parent resides upon determining that:</p>
          <p>(1) there is no motion for an order for adoptive placement of the child that has been filed or is reasonably anticipated by the responsible social services agency to be filed; and</p>
          <p>(2) filing the petition in the adopting parent's county of residence will expedite the proceedings and serve the best interests of the child.</p>
          <p>(c) When the court issues an order under paragraph (b), a copy of the court order shall be filed together with the adoption petition in the court of the adopting parent's county of residence.</p>
          <p>(d) The court shall notify the court conducting reviews under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a> when the adoption is finalized so that the court conducting reviews under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a> may close its jurisdiction and the court record, including the court's electronic case record, in the county conducting the reviews shall reflect that adoption of the child was finalized.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.621.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.36.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.623">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.623 ADOPTION PETITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.623.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Who may petition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The responsible social services agency may petition for the adopting parent to adopt a child who is under the guardianship of the commissioner. The petition shall contain or have attached a statement certified by the adopting parent that the adopting parent desires that the relationship of parent and child be established between the adopting parent and the child and that adoption is in the best interests of the child.</p>
          <p>(b) The adopting parent may petition the court for adoption of the child.</p>
          <p>(c) An adopting parent must be at least 21 years of age at the time the adoption petition is filed unless the adopting parent is an individual related to the child, as defined by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.01#stat.142B.01.15">142B.01, subdivision 15</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The petition may be filed in Minnesota by an adopting parent who resides within or outside the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.623.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Time for filing petition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An adoption petition shall be filed not later than nine months after the date of the fully executed adoption placement agreement unless the court finds that:</p>
          <p>(1) the time for filing a petition be extended because of the child's special needs as defined under title IV-E of the federal Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, section 672; or</p>
          <p>(2) based on a written plan for completing filing of the petition, including a specific timeline, to which the adopting parent has agreed, the time for filing a petition be extended long enough to complete the plan because an extension is in the best interests of the child and additional time is needed for the child to adjust to the adoptive home.</p>
          <p>(b) If an adoption petition is not filed within nine months of the execution of the adoption placement agreement as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.613#stat.260C.613.1">260C.613, subdivision 1</a>, and after giving the adopting parent written notice of its request together with the date and time of the hearing set to consider its report, the responsible social services agency shall file a report requesting an order for one of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) that the time for filing a petition be extended because of the child's special needs as defined under title IV-E of the federal Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, section 673;</p>
          <p>(2) that, based on a written plan for completing filing of the petition, including a specific timeline, to which the adopting parent has agreed, the time for filing a petition can be extended long enough to complete the plan because an extension is in the best interests of the child and additional time is needed for the child to adjust to the adoptive home; or</p>
          <p>(3) that the child can be removed from the adopting home.</p>
          <p>(c) At the conclusion of the review, the court shall issue findings, appropriate orders for the parties to take action or steps required to advance the case toward a finalized adoption, and set the date and time for the next review hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.623.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Requirements of petition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The petition shall be captioned in the legal name of the child as that name is reflected on the child's birth record prior to adoption and shall be entitled "Petition to Adopt Child under the Guardianship of the Commissioner of Children, Youth, and Families." The actual name of the child shall be supplied to the court by the responsible social services agency if unknown to the individual with whom the agency has made the adoptive placement.</p>
          <p>(b) The adoption petition shall be verified as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.141">260C.141</a>, subdivision 4, and, if filed by the responsible social services agency, signed and approved by the county attorney.</p>
          <p>(c) The petition shall state:</p>
          <p>(1) the full name, age, and place of residence of the adopting parent;</p>
          <p>(2) if the adopting parents are married, the date and place of marriage;</p>
          <p>(3) the date the adopting parent acquired physical custody of the child;</p>
          <p>(4) the date of the adoptive placement by the responsible social services agency;</p>
          <p>(5) the date of the birth of the child, if known, and the county, state, and country where born;</p>
          <p>(6) the name to be given the child, if a change of name is desired;</p>
          <p>(7) the description and value of any real or personal property owned by the child;</p>
          <p>(8) the relationship of the adopting parent to the child prior to adoptive placement, if any;</p>
          <p>(9) whether the Indian Child Welfare Act does or does not apply; and</p>
          <p>(10) the name and address of:</p>
          <p>(i) the child's guardian ad litem;</p>
          <p>(ii) the adoptee, if age ten or older;</p>
          <p>(iii) the child's Indian tribe, if the child is an Indian child; and</p>
          <p>(iv) the responsible social services agency.</p>
          <p>(d) A petition may ask for the adoption of two or more children.</p>
          <p>(e) If a petition is for adoption by a married person, both spouses must sign the petition indicating willingness to adopt the child and the petition must ask for adoption by both spouses unless the court approves adoption by only one spouse when spouses do not reside together or for other good cause shown.</p>
          <p>(f) If the petition is for adoption by a person residing outside the state, the adoptive placement must have been approved by the state where the person is a resident through the Interstate Compact on the Placement of Children, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.851">260.851</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.92">260.92</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.623.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Attachments to the petition.</span></h2>
          <p>The following must be filed with the petition:</p>
          <p>(1) the adoption study report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.41">259.41</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) the social and medical history required under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.43">259.43</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.609">260C.609</a>; and</p>
          <p>(3) a document prepared by the petitioner that establishes who must be given notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.627#stat.260C.627.1">260C.627, subdivision 1</a>, that includes the names and mailing addresses of those to be served by the court administrator.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.623.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.37.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.74.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 74</a>; art 8 s 70</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.625">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.625 DOCUMENTS FILED BY SOCIAL SERVICES AGENCY.</h1>
        <p>(a) The following shall be filed by the responsible social services agency prior to finalization of the adoption:</p>
        <p>(1) a certified copy of the child's birth record;</p>
        <p>(2) a certified copy of the findings and order terminating parental rights or order accepting the parent's consent to adoption under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.3">260C.515, subdivision 3</a>, and for guardianship to the commissioner;</p>
        <p>(3) a copy of any communication or contact agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.619">260C.619</a>;</p>
        <p>(4) certification that the Minnesota Fathers' Adoption Registry has been searched which requirement may be met according to the requirements of the Minnesota Rules of Adoption Procedure, Rule <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/court_rules/ju/subtype/radp/id/32/#32.01">32.01, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
        <p>(5) the original of each consent to adoption required, if any, unless the original was filed in the permanency proceeding conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.3">260C.515, subdivision 3</a>, and the order filed under clause (2) has a copy of the consent attached; and</p>
        <p>(6) the postplacement assessment report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.53#stat.259.53.2">259.53, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) The responsible social services agency shall provide any known aliases of the child to the court.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.625.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.38.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 38</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.627">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.627 NOTICE OF ADOPTION PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.627.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">To whom given.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notice of the adoption proceedings shall not be given to any parent whose rights have been terminated or who has consented to the adoption of the child under this chapter.</p>
          <p>(b) Notice of the adoption proceedings shall be given to the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the child's tribe if the child is an Indian child;</p>
          <p>(2) the responsible social services agency;</p>
          <p>(3) the child's guardian ad litem;</p>
          <p>(4) the child, if the child is age ten or over;</p>
          <p>(5) the child's attorney; and</p>
          <p>(6) the adopting parent.</p>
          <p>(c) Notice of a hearing regarding the adoption petition shall have a copy of the petition attached unless service of the petition has already been accomplished.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.627.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Method of service.</span></h2>
          <p>Notice of adoption proceedings for a child under the guardianship of the commissioner may be served by United States mail or any other method approved by the Minnesota Rules of Adoption Procedure.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.627.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.39.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 39</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.629">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.629 FINALIZATION HEARING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.629.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Consent.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A parent whose rights to the child have not been terminated must consent to the adoption of the child. A parent may consent to the adoption of the child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.3">260C.515, subdivision 3</a>, and that consent shall be irrevocable upon acceptance by the court except as otherwise provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a>, subdivision 3, clause (2)(i). A parent of an Indian child may consent to the adoption of the child according to United States Code, title 25, section 1913, and that consent may be withdrawn for any reason at any time before the entry of a final decree of adoption.</p>
          <p>(b) When the child to be adopted is age 14 years or older, the child's written consent to adoption by the adopting parent is required.</p>
          <p>(c) Consent by the responsible social services agency or the commissioner is not required because the adoptive placement has been made by the responsible social services agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.629.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required documents.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to issue a decree for adoption and enter judgment accordingly, the court must have the following documents in the record:</p>
          <p>(1) the original birth record of the child;</p>
          <p>(2) an adoption study report including a background study required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.41">259.41</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) a certified copy of the findings and order terminating parental rights or order accepting the parent's consent to adoption under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515#stat.260C.515.3">260C.515, subdivision 3</a>, and for guardianship to the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(4) any consents required under subdivision 1;</p>
          <p>(5) the child's social and medical history under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.609">260C.609</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) the postplacement assessment report required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.53#stat.259.53.2">259.53, subdivision 2</a>, unless waived by the court on the record at a hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a>; and</p>
          <p>(7) a report from the child's guardian ad litem.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.629.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.40.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 40</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.631">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.631 JUDGMENT AND DECREE.</h1>
        <p>(a) After taking testimony from the responsible social services agency, which may be by telephone or affidavit if the court has transferred venue of the matter to a county not conducting the posttermination of parental rights reviews under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a>, and the adopting parent, if the court finds that it is in the best interests of the child that the petition be granted, a decree of adoption shall be issued ordering that the child to be adopted shall be the child of the adopting parent. In the decree, the court may change the name of the adopted child, if a name change is requested.</p>
        <p>(b) After the decree is granted, the court administrator shall mail a copy of the decree to the commissioner of children, youth, and families.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.631.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.41.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.633">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.633 ADOPTION DENIED.</h1>
        <p>(a) If the court is not satisfied that the proposed adoption is in the best interests of the child to be adopted, the court shall deny the petition and order the responsible social services agency to take appropriate action for the protection and safety of the child. If venue has been transferred under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.621#stat.260C.621.2">260C.621, subdivision 2</a>, the court denying the petition shall notify the court originally conducting the guardianship reviews under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) The court responsible for conducting reviews under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.607">260C.607</a> shall set a hearing within 30 days of receiving notice of denial of the petition.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Any appeal of the denial of an adoption petition under this section shall be made according to the requirements of the Minnesota Rules of Adoption Procedure.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.633.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.42.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 42</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.635">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.635 EFFECT OF ADOPTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.635.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Legal effect.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Upon adoption, the adopted child becomes the legal child of the adopting parent and the adopting parent becomes the legal parent of the child with all the rights and duties between them of a birth parent and child.</p>
          <p>(b) The child shall inherit from the adoptive parent and the adoptive parent's relatives the same as though the child were the birth child of the parent, and in case of the child's death intestate, the adoptive parent and the adoptive parent's relatives shall inherit the child's estate as if the child had been the adoptive parent's birth child.</p>
          <p>(c) After a decree of adoption is entered, the birth parents or previous legal parents of the child shall be relieved of all parental responsibilities for the child except child support that has accrued to the date of the order for guardianship to the commissioner which continues to be due and owing. The child's birth or previous legal parent shall not exercise or have any rights over the adopted child or the adopted child's property, person, privacy, or reputation.</p>
          <p>(d) The adopted child shall not owe the birth parents or the birth parent's relatives any legal duty nor shall the adopted child inherit from the birth parents or kindred unless otherwise provided for in a will of the birth parent or kindred.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) Upon adoption, the court shall complete a certificate of adoption form and mail the form to the Office of Vital Records at the Minnesota Department of Health. Upon receiving the certificate of adoption, the state registrar shall register a replacement vital record in the new name of the adopted child as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.218">144.218</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.635.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Enrollment in American Indian tribe.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivision 1, the adoption of a child whose birth parent or parents are enrolled in an American Indian tribe shall not change the child's enrollment in that tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.635.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Communication or contact agreements.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not prohibit birth parents, relatives, birth or legal siblings, and adoptive parents from entering a communication or contact agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.619">260C.619</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.635.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.43.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.12.98.0">2013 c 108 art 12 s 98</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.260C.637"><b>260C.637</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.113.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 113</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">PLACEMENT OF CHILDREN IN QUALIFIED RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.70">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.70 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.70">260C.70</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.714">260C.714</a> may be cited as "Placements in Qualified Residential Treatment Programs." Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.70">260C.70</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.714">260C.714</a> implement the requirements of the Family First Prevention Services Act of 2018, Public Law 115-123, and apply to children for whom the juvenile treatment screening team under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.157#stat.260C.157.3">260C.157, subdivision 3</a>, recommends placement in a qualified residential treatment program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.84.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 84</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.702">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.702 REQUIREMENTS FOR PLACEMENTS IN QUALIFIED RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>For the responsible social services agency to place a child in a qualified residential treatment program, there must be:</p>
        <p>(1) an assessment by a qualified individual of whether it is necessary and appropriate to place the child at a qualified residential treatment program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) a family and permanency team under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.706">260C.706</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) an out-of-home placement plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a>;</p>
        <p>(4) court approval of a child's placement in a qualified residential treatment program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.71">260C.71</a>;</p>
        <p>(5) ongoing reviews and permanency hearings under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.712">260C.712</a>; and</p>
        <p>(6) a court review of any extended placement of the child in a qualified residential treatment program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.714">260C.714</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.702.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.85.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 85</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.704">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.704 REQUIREMENTS FOR THE QUALIFIED INDIVIDUAL'S ASSESSMENT OF THE CHILD FOR PLACEMENT IN A QUALIFIED RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) A qualified individual must complete an assessment of the child prior to the child's placement in a qualified residential treatment program in a format approved by the commissioner of human services unless, due to a crisis, the child must immediately be placed in a qualified residential treatment program. When a child must immediately be placed in a qualified residential treatment program without an assessment, the qualified individual must complete the child's assessment within 30 days of the child's placement. The qualified individual must:</p>
        <p>(1) assess the child's needs and strengths, using an age-appropriate, evidence-based, validated, functional assessment approved by the commissioner of human services;</p>
        <p>(2) determine whether the child's needs can be met by the child's family members or through placement in a family foster home; or, if not, determine which residential setting would provide the child with the most effective and appropriate level of care to the child in the least restrictive environment;</p>
        <p>(3) develop a list of short- and long-term mental and behavioral health goals for the child; and</p>
        <p>(4) work with the child's family and permanency team using culturally competent practices.</p>
        <p class="bl">If a level of care determination was conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245.4885">245.4885</a>, that information must be shared with the qualified individual and the juvenile treatment screening team.</p>
        <p>(b) The child and the child's parents, when appropriate, may request that a specific culturally competent qualified individual complete the child's assessment. The agency shall make efforts to refer the child to the identified qualified individual to complete the assessment. The assessment must not be delayed for a specific qualified individual to complete the assessment.</p>
        <p>(c) The qualified individual must provide the assessment, when complete, to the responsible social services agency. If the assessment recommends placement of the child in a qualified residential treatment facility, the agency must distribute the assessment to the child's parent or legal guardian and file the assessment with the court report as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.71">260C.71</a>, subdivision 2. If the assessment does not recommend placement in a qualified residential treatment facility, the agency must provide a copy of the assessment to the parents or legal guardians and the guardian ad litem and file the assessment determination with the court at the next required hearing as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.71#stat.260C.71.5">260C.71, subdivision 5</a>. If court rules and chapter 13 permit disclosure of the results of the child's assessment, the agency may share the results of the child's assessment with the child's foster care provider, other members of the child's family, and the family and permanency team. The agency must not share the child's private medical data with the family and permanency team unless: (1) chapter 13 permits the agency to disclose the child's private medical data to the family and permanency team; or (2) the child's parent has authorized the agency to disclose the child's private medical data to the family and permanency team.</p>
        <p>(d) For an Indian child, the assessment of the child must follow the order of placement preferences in the Indian Child Welfare Act of 1978, United States Code, title 25, section 1915.</p>
        <p>(e) In the assessment determination, the qualified individual must specify in writing:</p>
        <p>(1) the reasons why the child's needs cannot be met by the child's family or in a family foster home. A shortage of family foster homes is not an acceptable reason for determining that a family foster home cannot meet a child's needs;</p>
        <p>(2) why the recommended placement in a qualified residential treatment program will provide the child with the most effective and appropriate level of care to meet the child's needs in the least restrictive environment possible and how placing the child at the treatment program is consistent with the short-term and long-term goals of the child's permanency plan; and</p>
        <p>(3) if the qualified individual's placement recommendation is not the placement setting that the parent, family and permanency team, child, or tribe prefer, the qualified individual must identify the reasons why the qualified individual does not recommend the parent's, family and permanency team's, child's, or tribe's placement preferences. The out-of-home placement plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a> must also include reasons why the qualified individual did not recommend the preferences of the parents, family and permanency team, child, or tribe.</p>
        <p>(f) If the qualified individual determines that the child's family or a family foster home or other less restrictive placement may meet the child's needs, the agency must move the child out of the qualified residential treatment program and transition the child to a less restrictive setting within 30 days of the determination. If the responsible social services agency has placement authority of the child, the agency must make a plan for the child's placement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>. The agency must file the child's assessment determination with the court at the next required hearing.</p>
        <p>(g) If the qualified individual recommends placing the child in a qualified residential treatment program and if the responsible social services agency has placement authority of the child, the agency shall make referrals to appropriate qualified residential treatment programs and, upon acceptance by an appropriate program, place the child in an approved or certified qualified residential treatment program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.704.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.86.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 86</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.43.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 43</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.706">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.706 FAMILY AND PERMANENCY TEAM REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) When the responsible social services agency's juvenile treatment screening team, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.157">260C.157</a>, recommends placing the child in a qualified residential treatment program, the agency must assemble a family and permanency team within ten days.</p>
        <p>(1) The team must include all appropriate biological family members, the child's parents, legal guardians or custodians, foster care providers, and relatives as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007, subdivisions 26b</a> and 27, and professionals, as appropriate, who are a resource to the child's family, such as teachers, medical or mental health providers, or clergy.</p>
        <p>(2) When a child is placed in foster care prior to the qualified residential treatment program, the agency shall include relatives responding to the relative search notice as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a> on this team, unless the juvenile court finds that contacting a specific relative would present a safety or health risk to the parent, guardian, child, sibling, or any other family member.</p>
        <p>(3) When a qualified residential treatment program is the child's initial placement setting, the responsible social services agency must engage with the child and the child's parents to determine the appropriate family and permanency team members.</p>
        <p>(4) When the permanency goal is to reunify the child with the child's parent or legal guardian, the purpose of the relative search and focus of the family and permanency team is to preserve family relationships and identify and develop supports for the child and parents.</p>
        <p>(5) The responsible agency must make a good faith effort to identify and assemble all appropriate individuals to be part of the child's family and permanency team and request input from the parents regarding relative search efforts consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>. The out-of-home placement plan in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a> must include all contact information for the team members, as well as contact information for family members or relatives who are not a part of the family and permanency team.</p>
        <p>(6) If the child is age 14 or older, the team must include members of the family and permanency team that the child selects in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(7) Consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a>, a responsible social services agency may disclose relevant and appropriate private data about the child to relatives in order for the relatives to participate in caring and planning for the child's placement.</p>
        <p>(8) If the child is an Indian child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.751">260.751</a>, the responsible social services agency must make active efforts to include the child's tribal representative on the family and permanency team.</p>
        <p>(b) The family and permanency team shall meet regarding the assessment required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a> to determine whether it is necessary and appropriate to place the child in a qualified residential treatment program and to participate in case planning under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) When reunification of the child with the child's parent or legal guardian is the permanency plan, the family and permanency team shall support the parent-child relationship by recognizing the parent's legal authority, consulting with the parent regarding ongoing planning for the child, and assisting the parent with visiting and contacting the child.</p>
        <p>(d) When the agency's permanency plan is to transfer the child's permanent legal and physical custody to a relative or for the child's adoption, the team shall:</p>
        <p>(1) coordinate with the proposed guardian to provide the child with educational services, medical care, and dental care;</p>
        <p>(2) coordinate with the proposed guardian, the agency, and the foster care facility to meet the child's treatment needs after the child is placed in a permanent placement with the proposed guardian;</p>
        <p>(3) plan to meet the child's need for safety, stability, and connection with the child's family and community after the child is placed in a permanent placement with the proposed guardian; and</p>
        <p>(4) in the case of an Indian child, communicate with the child's tribe to identify necessary and appropriate services for the child, transition planning for the child, the child's treatment needs, and how to maintain the child's connections to the child's community, family, and tribe.</p>
        <p>(e) The agency shall invite the family and permanency team to participate in case planning and the agency shall give the team notice of court reviews under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.152">260C.152</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.221">260C.221</a> until: (1) the child is reunited with the child's parents; or (2) the child's foster care placement ends and the child is in a permanent placement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.706.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.87.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.44.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 44</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.708">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.708 OUT-OF-HOME PLACEMENT PLAN FOR QUALIFIED RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT PROGRAM PLACEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) When the responsible social services agency places a child in a qualified residential treatment program as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.26d">260C.007, subdivision 26d</a>, the out-of-home placement plan must include:</p>
        <p>(1) the case plan requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) the reasonable and good faith efforts of the responsible social services agency to identify and include all of the individuals required to be on the child's family and permanency team under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>;</p>
        <p>(3) all contact information for members of the child's family and permanency team and for other relatives who are not part of the family and permanency team;</p>
        <p>(4) evidence that the agency scheduled meetings of the family and permanency team, including meetings relating to the assessment required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a>, at a time and place convenient for the family;</p>
        <p>(5) evidence that the family and permanency team is involved in the assessment required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a> to determine the appropriateness of the child's placement in a qualified residential treatment program;</p>
        <p>(6) the family and permanency team's placement preferences for the child in the assessment required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a>. When making a decision about the child's placement preferences, the family and permanency team must recognize:</p>
        <p>(i) that the agency should place a child with the child's siblings unless a court finds that placing a child with the child's siblings is not possible due to a child's specialized placement needs or is otherwise contrary to the child's best interests; and</p>
        <p>(ii) that the agency should place an Indian child according to the requirements of the Indian Child Welfare Act, the Minnesota Family Preservation Act under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.751">260.751 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.835">260.835</a>, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.193#stat.260C.193.3">260C.193, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (g);</p>
        <p>(7) when reunification of the child with the child's parent or legal guardian is the agency's goal, evidence demonstrating that the parent or legal guardian provided input about the members of the family and permanency team under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.706">260C.706</a>;</p>
        <p>(8) when the agency's permanency goal is to reunify the child with the child's parent or legal guardian, the out-of-home placement plan must identify services and supports that maintain the parent-child relationship and the parent's legal authority, decision-making, and responsibility for ongoing planning for the child. In addition, the agency must assist the parent with visiting and contacting the child;</p>
        <p>(9) when the agency's permanency goal is to transfer permanent legal and physical custody of the child to a proposed guardian or to finalize the child's adoption, the case plan must document the agency's steps to transfer permanent legal and physical custody of the child or finalize adoption, as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), clauses (6) and (7); and</p>
        <p>(10) the qualified individual's recommendation regarding the child's placement in a qualified residential treatment program and the court approval or disapproval of the placement as required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.71">260C.71</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) If the placement preferences of the family and permanency team, child, and tribe, if applicable, are not consistent with the placement setting that the qualified individual recommends, the case plan must include the reasons why the qualified individual did not recommend following the preferences of the family and permanency team, child, and the tribe.</p>
        <p>(c) The agency must file the out-of-home placement plan with the court as part of the 60-day court order under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.71">260C.71</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.708.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.88.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 88</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.45.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 45</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.71">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.71 COURT APPROVAL REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.71.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Judicial review.</span></h2>
          <p>When the responsible social services agency has legal authority to place a child at a qualified residential treatment facility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 21a, and the child's assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a> recommends placing the child in a qualified residential treatment facility, the agency shall place the child at a qualified residential facility. Within 60 days of placing the child at a qualified residential treatment facility, the agency must obtain a court order finding that the child's placement is appropriate and meets the child's individualized needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.71.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Qualified residential treatment program; agency report to court.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The responsible social services agency shall file a written report with the court after receiving the qualified individual's assessment as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a> prior to the child's placement or within 35 days of the date of the child's placement in a qualified residential treatment facility. The written report shall contain or have attached:</p>
          <p>(1) the child's name, date of birth, race, gender, and current address;</p>
          <p>(2) the names, races, dates of birth, residence, and post office address of the child's parents or legal custodian or guardian;</p>
          <p>(3) the name and address of the qualified residential treatment program, including a chief administrator of the facility;</p>
          <p>(4) a statement of the facts that necessitated the child's foster care placement;</p>
          <p>(5) the child's out-of-home placement plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>, including the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) if the child is placed in an out-of-state qualified residential treatment program, the compelling reasons why the child's needs cannot be met by an in-state placement;</p>
          <p>(7) the qualified individual's assessment of the child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a>, paragraph (c), in a format approved by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(8) if, at the time required for the report under this subdivision, the child's parent or legal guardian, a child who is ten years of age or older, the family and permanency team, or a tribe disagrees with the recommended qualified residential treatment program placement, information regarding the disagreement and to the extent possible, the basis for the disagreement in the report; and</p>
          <p>(9) any other information that the responsible social services agency, child's parent, legal custodian or guardian, child, or, in the case of an Indian child, tribe would like the court to consider.</p>
          <p>(b) The agency shall file the written report under paragraph (a) with the court and serve on the parties a request for a hearing or a court order without a hearing.</p>
          <p>(c) The agency must inform the child's parent or legal guardian and a child who is ten years of age or older of the court review requirements of this section and the child and child's parent's or legal guardian's right to submit information to the court:</p>
          <p>(1) the agency must inform the child's parent or legal guardian and a child who is ten years of age or older of the reporting date and the date by which the agency must receive information from the child and child's parent so that the agency is able to submit the report required by this subdivision to the court;</p>
          <p>(2) the agency must inform the child's parent or legal guardian and a child who is ten years of age or older that the court will hold a hearing upon the request of the child or the child's parent; and</p>
          <p>(3) the agency must inform the child's parent or legal guardian and a child who is ten years of age or older that they have the right to request a hearing and the right to present information to the court for the court's review under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.71.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Court hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The court shall hold a hearing when a party or a child who is ten years of age or older requests a hearing.</p>
          <p>(b) In all other circumstances, the court has the discretion to hold a hearing or issue an order without a hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.71.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Court findings and order.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 60 days from the beginning of each placement in a qualified residential treatment program when the qualified individual's assessment of the child recommends placing the child in a qualified residential treatment program, the court must consider the qualified individual's assessment of the child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a> and issue an order to:</p>
          <p>(1) determine whether a family foster home can meet the child's needs, whether it is necessary and appropriate to place a child in a qualified residential treatment program that is the least restrictive environment possible, and whether the child's placement is consistent with the child's short and long term goals as specified in the permanency plan; and</p>
          <p>(2) approve or disapprove of the child's placement.</p>
          <p>(b) If the court disapproves of the child's placement in a qualified residential treatment program, the responsible social services agency shall: (1) remove the child from the qualified residential treatment program within 30 days of the court's order; and (2) make a plan for the child's placement that is consistent with the child's best interests under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.71.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Court review and approval not required.</span></h2>
          <p>When the responsible social services agency has legal authority to place a child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007#stat.260C.007.21a">260C.007, subdivision 21a</a>, and the qualified individual's assessment of the child does not recommend placing the child in a qualified residential treatment program, the court is not required to hold a hearing and the court is not required to issue an order. Pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a>, paragraph (f), the responsible social services agency shall make a plan for the child's placement consistent with the child's best interests under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.2">260C.212, subdivision 2</a>. The agency must file the agency's assessment determination for the child with the court at the next required hearing.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.89.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.46.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.712">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.712 ONGOING REVIEWS AND PERMANENCY HEARING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>As long as a child remains placed in a qualified residential treatment program, the responsible social services agency shall submit evidence at each administrative review under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a>; each court review under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.202">260C.202</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.203">260C.203</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.204">260C.204</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260D.06">260D.06</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260D.07">260D.07</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260D.08">260D.08</a>; and each permanency hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.515">260C.515</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.519">260C.519</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.521">260C.521</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260D.07">260D.07</a> that:</p>
        <p>(1) demonstrates that an ongoing assessment of the strengths and needs of the child continues to support the determination that the child's needs cannot be met through placement in a family foster home;</p>
        <p>(2) demonstrates that the placement of the child in a qualified residential treatment program provides the most effective and appropriate level of care for the child in the least restrictive environment;</p>
        <p>(3) demonstrates how the placement is consistent with the short-term and long-term goals for the child, as specified in the child's permanency plan;</p>
        <p>(4) documents how the child's specific treatment or service needs will be met in the placement;</p>
        <p>(5) documents the length of time that the agency expects the child to need treatment or services;</p>
        <p>(6) documents the responsible social services agency's efforts to prepare the child to return home or to be placed with a fit and willing relative, legal guardian, adoptive parent, or foster family; and</p>
        <p>(7) if the child is placed in a qualified residential treatment program out of state, documents the compelling reasons for placing the child out of state, and the reasons that the child's needs cannot be met by an in-state placement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.712.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.90.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 90</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.47.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.714">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.714 REVIEW OF EXTENDED QUALIFIED RESIDENTIAL TREATMENT PROGRAM PLACEMENTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) When a responsible social services agency places a child in a qualified residential treatment program for more than 12 consecutive months or 18 nonconsecutive months or, in the case of a child who is under 13 years of age, for more than six consecutive or nonconsecutive months, the agency must submit: (1) the signed approval by the county social services director of the responsible social services agency; and (2) the evidence supporting the child's placement at the most recent court review or permanency hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.712">260C.712</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner shall specify the procedures and requirements for the agency's review and approval of a child's extended qualified residential treatment program placement. The commissioner may consult with counties, tribes, child-placing agencies, mental health providers, licensed facilities, the child, the child's parents, and the family and permanency team members to develop case plan requirements and engage in periodic reviews of the case plan.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.260C.714.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.91.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 91</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.48.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 48</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.260C.80">
        <h1 class="shn">260C.80 OFFICE OF THE FOSTER YOUTH OMBUDSPERSON; BOARD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.260C.80.1">
